## 12. SEC3 day-two amendment

Implementer: SEC3, 2026-10-19. Commission: day-two fix list 3/4/5/8.
REL5 owns release transitions and compatibility; MIG owns backfills and staging
clock control. This amendment changes security analysis, not runtime authority.

### 12.1 Column read intent

`invariant table.column read only P` protects the value, computations over it,
value-dependent counts/existence/refusals, copied values, assets and delivery.
It does not forbid observing an otherwise public row's existence independently
of the protected value. Binding validates the column and preserves its identity
in every verdict and audit. Effective payload authority is row read AND any
owner-private field gate. Pure projections excluding the field may eliminate
that payload path; predicates, group/order keys, derived values and copied
fields remain observations. Unknown flow stays unproved. A synthetic whole-row
read cannot refute a projection-only column policy: column counterexamples
execute an authored observer in two schema-valid worlds differing only in that
column, with policy false in both. Outputs must differ reproducibly. Maintained
values are recomputed; column-sensitive counts/existence are included. Separate
obligations name unresolved raw-probe, wire, device and opaque flows. A direct
column theorem never silently clears those obligations. Per-column audit
reports effective audience plus operation/derived observers, not only its type.

### 12.2 Reproducible campaigns and reachability

Generated seed ingress contains only authored base rows/fields. Never re-seed
maintained targets or engine-recorded fields. Generated lifetime samples are
live at the campaign clock; principal fields vary independently. Rejected
snapshots are recorded as unsupported, not successful attempts. Existing
fixture histories instead fork the complete SQLite store and asset ledger into
owned isolated memory stores; captures, deadlines, maintained values, lifetimes
and actual media identities survive unchanged. No source store is modified.
Temporary asset directories belong to Store and disappear on drop.

Actors include all principal values in fixtures. Required/optional references
range over fixture rows, not only the first. Short histories visit first steps
breadth first before extending them under a reported work cap. Read and row
assertions are checked after each step. Write/operation assertions judge only
actual committed changes, correct insert/update/delete arms and pre-write
policy state; ambiguous multiple writes, implicit effects or unstable policy
need executed-effect tracing and remain unsupported. A finite trace is never
an induction proof for always or arbitrary histories.

Every counterexample has a machine-readable origin: authored trace from trusted
fixture, trusted-seed-only, or synthetic operation. Only the first establishes
application reachability from the recorded trusted starting state and fails
normal check/test. Seed/synthetic witnesses remain refutations of all-snapshot
claims, visible with --all and JSON, without failing acceptance. Unproved
reachability is never asserted unreachable as a mathematical theorem. Check
loads the same fixture base for reachable histories as test, without HTTP or
business-case execution. A stronger witness must replace a synthetic witness
for the same declaration. --all exposes all witnesses and never changes which
ones fail. No unreachable refutation may print pass/ok beside exit 1.

### 12.3 Actionable authority advice

N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION identifies authored caller operations that write role
or group grants trusted by later rules while the effective admission does not
establish independent authority to confer that particular grant. Include
self-signup roles and transitions where a caller can confer a role they lack.
A trusted service seat or an independently authorized administrator may appoint
other roles; holding an unrelated ordinary role does not establish delegation.
Advice names the operation, grant and gate and points to guide staging's
operator first-admin recipe. It is advice, never an implicit runtime refusal
or a proof of malicious intent. Legitimate open enrollment can be intentional.

N_GRANT_OUTLIVES_ROLE requires an actually mutable/revocable current-role
population and a retained principal/grant branch lacking that role recheck;
cover read AND write branches. Immutable/static role populations do not fire.
N_EXISTENCE_ORACLE excludes public populations and caller-owned role lookups;
only an absent/hidden distinction crossing a narrower private audience warrants
advice. Every security note has a real source location and contributes to the
reported note count. Rule printing preserves connective precedence. Identity
429 responses include a conservative retry delay covering the rate window;
the existing limits and authentication semantics are unchanged.

### 12.4 Evidence

Qualification uses read-only copies of all ten wave-17 high lanes and their
spec-authored invariant sets, plus all 150 earlier schemas. Report caught,
partial and not caught separately; all-snapshot witnesses alone do not count
as a reachable detection. Preserve bad/fixed controls, note counts and source
hashes. The open qualification-gap ticket remains open for unresolved whole
proof/device/opaque obligations. No zero-hole claim follows from finite tests.


### 12.5 SEC3 reduction details

The direct column theorem is `(row_read AND owner_private_gate) => P` in the
existing three-valued kernel; deny-delete is never an admission for column
read. Whole proof still requires every observer obligation. This increment
uses dynamic column-world observers, not an unimplemented static dataflow
claim: pure projections can avoid a refutation, but do not acquire a proof by
absence of finite counterexamples. Census is existing point/scan/view/filter/
count/sum/exists, mutation return/refusal/copy, asset, sync, raw/derived/opaque
paths. Unsupported field flow remains unproved. Audit's `column_audiences`
maps every visible column name to its effective row-and-private-field rule;
operations and derived edges remain separately enumerated.

Column worlds use the same ordinary viewer, server clock and row id; the named
column has two different schema-valid typed values, and policy is false on its
respective image in each committed world. Every non-native authored query and
deterministic mutation is eligible; fresh ids, effects and incomplete/resource
results are declined. Compare typed logical data and authored refusal messages,
retain ordered arrays, repeat both observations to reject unstable outputs.
Rebuild maintained rows from authored inputs, never accept fabricated derived
or recorded fields. Required media substitutions without a coupled real ledger
remain explicitly unsupported. A primary observation from the exact trusted
fixture is a reachable observation; its synthetic alternate world establishes
value dependency, not reachability of an alternate application history. JSON
retains both worlds and explicitly states this distinction.

`AuthoredFixtureTrace`, `TrustedSeedOnly`, `SyntheticOperation` are the witness
origins (serialized in kebab-case). Status remains refuted for an all-snapshot
counterexample. Normal text labels seed/synthetic refutations non-failing and
hides their large witnesses; --all reveals them and does not change exit status.
Only authored fixture observations fail check/test. A reachable witness wins
over a synthetic witness for the same declaration. With no fixture, check uses
schema-only exploration and names missing application-reachability coverage.
Check discovers an explicit --fixture or project fixtures/; it uses an isolated
in-memory loader at the fixed campaign clock, never the development store.

Candidates sort by operation, argument value and principal. Enumerate bounded
Cartesian arguments including nullable enums and fixture references; interleave
operations for breadth-first first steps, then diagonal ordered pairs. The
512 first-step/512-pair and 1000-fixture-row caps are reported; no exhaustive
claim is made for unvisited combinations. Fixture asset directories are owned
by Store and removed on drop, including error returns. Histories judge direct
single-write net images only; multiple writes, dependency-changing and opaque
boundaries remain unsupported rather than fabricating write-boundary events.

The escalation advisory compares role privileges structurally, never by an
administrator role-name allowlist. Enumerate literals from a role enum or
role probes over a text roster; substitute each caller/assigned role into the
roster write gates and other table-effect gates. A possible ordinary assignment
that adds an effect the caller lacks warrants advice; explicit service-only
branches are excluded from this advisory cast, not from invariant proofs.
Literal assignment targets restrict the target set; dynamic values retain all
candidate roles. This is overapproximate advice: independent operation guards,
intentional enrollment and delegation still need specification review.
Grant-lifetime notes require a role/kind column with an authored update/upsert/
delete and a mutable role field; immutable seed-only populations are excluded.
Existence advice requires a private table lookup, an independent stricter read
policy and distinct later refusal; public and viewer-owned lookups are excluded.
Locations are exact column/rule sites where available, table sites otherwise;
the project-wide missing-intent note uses the first source's line 1.

Identity rate refusals on the existing identity routes use the existing
60-second WINDOW: Retry-After: 60, denied.retryAfterMs: 60000. This is a
conservative wait, not exposure of individual rate-key state. No limit changes.
Qualification inputs are ~/sb4-loop/wave17-repro/sec/<lane>/snapback/security.q
and read-only wave17 app copies; the report pins their hashes. The ten high
lanes are 006/011/013/015/019/021/027/040/045/048. Caught means an authored
fixture observation of the reported bad behavior; partial retains a relevant
note or synthetic witness with a missing affected path. Each of the eight real
highs is scored separately. All eight caught and no false refusals on 150 older
schemas is the target, never inferred from an incomplete run.


### 12.6 Disclosure and clock clarifications

A column observer mutation is declined if it can change the protected image,
policy dependency or an unresolved implicit writer before disclosure. A
pre-operation false policy cannot refute an authorized post-transfer return.
Column observers also run after committed history steps, preserving the
trusted initial fixture and authored prefix. All reference and principal
arguments use the same bounded fixture Cartesian cases; truncation of read
observer cases at 32 is named. Clock-only whole-row probes exclude columns.
Column tick coverage beyond the recorded campaign clock remains named.

All security fixture loading, probes, writes, paired deletions and policy
judgments use the same explicit campaign clock. Business tests keep their own
existing clock and store; security reloads trusted fixture files independently.
Raw policy probes respect both not-before and expire-at bounds. Direct delete
assertions judge removed pre-images against the stable pre-write state, never
surviving rows alone; ambiguous implicit removals remain unsupported.

Fixture forks copy already activated engine state without re-adopting a schema.
They use fresh entropy/minter and private assets, never shared live connections.
Ephemeral-hub fixtures require a separate isolated snapshot and are declined.
Structural privilege advice combines dominating safe operation requirements
with the actual insert/update/upsert gate and applicable owned-field patch gate.
A mutable roster alone is not enough for lifetime advice: require independent
current-role intent or a retained recipient column tied to a role literal
(judge, inspector, host, contractor, engineer; ordinary author attribution alone
is not a current-role contract). Deletion revokes even an immutable role field.
# Security invariants

State privacy intent separately from enforcement. Check reports proved, refuted
with a replayed counterexample, or unproved with a reason; passing finite tests
are not proofs.

```quarry
table privateNotes:
  patient: principal
  body: text 1..2000
  read <- .patient = viewer
  insert <- .patient = viewer
  online only
invariant privateNotes.read only .patient = viewer
invariant privateNotes.body read only .patient = viewer
invariant privateNotes.insert only .patient = viewer
query privateNote(note: privateNotes):
  return privateNotes[note]
mutation writePrivateNote(body: text 1..2000):
  return insert privateNotes { patient: viewer, body }
```

Run `snapback4 check`, `snapback4 audit` (or `check --audit`), then
`snapback4 test`. `audit --json` lists conditional row predicates, operations,
columns, inherited grants and grant writers. Its final section lists conditional role/row/effect exposures with no matching invariant; declaring one effect does not cover the others. `check` names broad signed-in
reads and missing intent, once per check for the project. Review `snapback/generated/security-invariants.q.draft` against the specification; it mirrors existing rules and can repeat the same mistakes. Confirm or correct each candidate and copy chosen declarations into an authored `.q` file. The draft is never compiled or auto-accepted. The test command runs isolated schema-derived
challenges automatically. Replayed authored observations from trusted fixtures fail acceptance; seed-only and synthetic witnesses carry separate reachability labels, remain in JSON, and are shown in text with `--all`. They do not fail check/test. Coverage
that needs a live HTTP server, fixture assets, operation sequences or a device
is named as not covered; zero violations alone does not qualify an app secure.

## Reference

`invariant table.column read only P` protects a column value, derived values and value-dependent counts/existence/refusals. An authored projection excluding the column does not by itself disclose that value; filtering, ordering or copying it can. Audit JSON includes `column_audiences` (effective row rule plus private-owner gate). Unknown derived and opaque paths remain unproved.

`invariant table.read only P` protects read intent; insert, update and delete
name authority. `invariant table.column write only P` covers writes to a
column (both insert and update). `invariant table always P` states row integrity.
An update policy over `.field` constrains **both old and next images**.
A policy saying status must not be override therefore also restricts editing
a row that was already override, even when changing it away from that status.
For the role authority of one named mutation, use operation-specific intent
(`invariant table.operation only P`) instead. General next-image-only and
cross-operation transition assertions still need a separate reduction.
Predicates use the existing rule vocabulary: `.field`, `viewer`, `exists`,
comparisons, `and`, `or`, `not`. For a role write use the complete roster probe,
for example `invariant members.role write only exists roles[viewer, role = 'owner']`
in a schema declaring those tables. A declaration never grants authority.

The implemented proof fragment exhaustively checks independent three-valued
rule predicates. A failed implication is unproved until an engine replay
establishes a counterexample. A schema-valid seeded snapshot is not a claim
that an application operation sequence reaches that state. Required-column
non-null assertions have a separate validation theorem. Read/count/sum/existence obligations are reported separately. A maintained aggregate with bare privacy keys can prove its contributor audience; a broader aggregate can yield a paired present/absent counterexample. Exact server `not before`/`expire at` bounds can prove a temporal read component independently of physical sweeps. General transitions and unsupported expressions remain unproved.
Read-through captures/live parents, complex derived aggregates, raw rule probes,
owned-field update bypasses, cascade/set-null and opaque jobs/workers need
separate reductions. An unresolved path blocks the whole declaration's proof.

Server delivery authorizes against the current server snapshot. A disconnected
device can retain an earlier snapshot until it receives a revocation; even
`until-revoked` cannot know a later server role. Synced current-role intent is
therefore unproved for device access. Retained history follows its leave policy.
These reports exclude physical timing and resource/meter side channels and
explicitly exclude the trusted system-principal boundary. Service seats remain
inside the ordinary-viewer check; there is no implicit seat exemption.

## Current roles and derived grants

A membership or assignment created while a user is an inspector can survive
their demotion. Conjoin the **current reader's role** with the assignment
branch, or revoke every derived grant transactionally whenever the role changes.
A grant writer's role does not establish the recipient's current authority.
The grant-lifetime advice points at this pattern; the argument-grant advice identifies
arguments written into reader-grant columns: validate the chosen principal's
role and relationship, even when the caller is privileged.
Global admin authority must come from a trusted operator or existing admin;
a group-creation `created` probe must remain scoped to that group everywhere.
`N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION` identifies role assignments trusted by later rules without independent delegation. Open enrollment may be intentional: compare each note with the specification. See `guide staging` for operator first-admin setup and `guide sharing` for parent authority.

## Required evidence and deadlines

If the spec requires a photo, use a required `image <=5000000` column or
`[image <=5000000] 1..4`; `?` permits omission and `[image] <=4` permits zero.
Uploads are verified media and asset reads inherit row and private-field rules.
Optional-evidence advice asks the builder to check intentional optionality.
Text `min..max` counts non-whitespace Unicode characters toward the minimum;
the maximum counts all characters. Stored text is not trimmed or rewritten.
A sweep changes state eventually: compare the deadline with server `now` in
the mutation too if writes must stop exactly at that instant.

## Existence observations

A lookup that says NOT_FOUND on absence followed by a denial saying FORBIDDEN
can reveal existence to callers admitted by a broader read rule. Use the same
code **and message** for hidden/absent/denied cases when existence is private,
or tighten the table read rule. Program `exists` is caller-filtered; probes
inside table rules inspect raw rows and can disclose predicates indirectly.
Inspect both paths in `audit`; a payload-only read check is insufficient.

## When an observer remains unproved

The report names the edge that lacks a reduction. Preserve the specification
while changing its enforcement; weakening an invariant to match a leaking
rule does not repair privacy.

- Maintained counts/sums: retain privacy fields as ordinary bare group keys
  and protect the aggregate with the same audience predicate. Recorded
  reference paths, calendar keys, derived values, private columns and temporal
  contributors need their own reduction; their names are not source fields.
- Owner fields: declare column-write intent too. `written by` authorizes
  patches even when the ordinary update rule denies. To prove a particular
  mutation's role authority, use a dominating current-role requirement and
  operation-specific intent over a role table the mutation cannot change.
  This does not prove every owner-field or old/next-image path.
- Raw rule probes: inspect the observing table and its writers. A protected
  target's read rule does not protect predicates probed from another rule.
  Apply the intended audience at that observer, or remove the raw dependency;
  the report retains the unsupported edge until it can establish implication.
- Cascade/set-null: automatic child changes bypass child write rules. An
  explicit authorized child mutation with `restrict` removes that automatic
  writer, but parent refusal/existence and transitive references remain
  separate observations. Read-through/live-parent authority requires a separate reduction.
- Jobs/native workers/actions: the audit names the trusted or opaque writer.
  An ordinary mutation with stable role requirements can prove that mutation's
  authority; arbitrary native/worker output cannot acquire a proof from a
  caller's role or a passing finite test.
- Schedules: use a stored `not before`/`expire at` lifetime for exact read
  boundaries, or compare server `now` in the write itself. A scheduled status
  update leaves the old status visible between deadline and sweep.

Default `test` challenges pure-schema applications through a private production
`serve`: unauthenticated calls, forged persona headers and development doors.
The host uses no pre-existing developer credentials or development mode. Applications needing
native/worker artifacts are explicitly not covered by that schema-only host.
Focused fixtures also enable short ordered mutation histories followed by
ordinary reads; traces record actual calls and current policy evaluation.
Named personas/service seats and enum argument combinations are enumerated
within reported limits. Asset challenges use real foreign-upload and verified
non-image ledgers; video arguments and unvisited histories remain named. Complete fixture stores preserve image/file ledgers, captures, read-through deadlines, maintained views and expiry. Histories include fixture principals and reference-row candidates, under a reported work cap. Neither
these finite campaigns nor an offline device's retained snapshot establishes
current-server revocation at a disconnected access.

### E_SECURITY_TARGET

E_SECURITY_TARGET means the invariant target does not name a declared table, column or effect. Correct the target name; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_SECURITY_INTENT

N_SECURITY_INTENT means access rules lack independent declared intent. Declare policy from the spec; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_BROAD_READ

N_BROAD_READ identifies universal or roster-wide readership; its message states the exact condition. Confirm the intended audience; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_GRANT_OUTLIVES_ROLE

N_GRANT_OUTLIVES_ROLE means a stored grant may survive a later role change. Recheck the current reader role; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_ARGUMENT_GRANT

N_ARGUMENT_GRANT means an argument becomes a reader-grant column. Validate the recipient role and relationship; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_OPTIONAL_EVIDENCE

N_OPTIONAL_EVIDENCE means optional media permits omitted evidence. Declare required media when the spec requires it; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_SWEEP_ONLY_DEADLINE

N_SWEEP_ONLY_DEADLINE means sweeps leave time between a deadline and cleanup. Enforce the deadline in the mutation; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_EXISTENCE_ORACLE

N_EXISTENCE_ORACLE means absence and later denial can reveal different outcomes. Unify the code and message when existence is private; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

The isolated production HTTP campaign also signs in, refreshes the session,
logs out all sessions and retries both retired credentials against query,
mutation, schema, changes, sync, probe and refresh doors. It reports missing coverage
when ordinary identity or complete deployed worker artifacts are unavailable.
This checks server admission; a disconnected device can still retain old bytes.

### N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION

N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION identifies self-signup roles or role-grant operations that can confer authority trusted by other rules. Restrict assignment to authorized delegation; provision the first administrator with `guide staging`.

Identity rate refusals return HTTP `Retry-After` (seconds) and
`denied.retryAfterMs` (milliseconds), conservatively covering the 60-second
window. Wait that interval before retrying identity requests.

REL5 owns release transitions/old-client compatibility; MIG owns backfills and
staging clock control for day-two qualification.

## 12. SEC3 day-two amendment

Implementer: SEC3, 2026-10-19. Commission: day-two fix list 3/4/5/8.
REL5 owns release transitions and compatibility; MIG owns backfills and staging
clock control. This amendment changes security analysis, not runtime authority.

### 12.1 Column read intent

`invariant table.column read only P` protects the value, computations over it,
value-dependent counts/existence/refusals, copied values, assets and delivery.
It does not forbid observing an otherwise public row's existence independently
of the protected value. Binding validates the column and preserves its identity
in every verdict and audit. Effective payload authority is row read AND any
owner-private field gate. Pure projections excluding the field may eliminate
that payload path; predicates, group/order keys, derived values and copied
fields remain observations. Unknown flow stays unproved. A synthetic whole-row
read cannot refute a projection-only column policy: column counterexamples
execute an authored observer in two schema-valid worlds differing only in that
column, with policy false in both. Outputs must differ reproducibly. Maintained
values are recomputed; column-sensitive counts/existence are included. Separate
obligations name unresolved raw-probe, wire, device and opaque flows. A direct
column theorem never silently clears those obligations. Per-column audit
reports effective audience plus operation/derived observers, not only its type.

### 12.2 Reproducible campaigns and reachability

Generated seed ingress contains only authored base rows/fields. Never re-seed
maintained targets or engine-recorded fields. Generated lifetime samples are
live at the campaign clock; principal fields vary independently. Rejected
snapshots are recorded as unsupported, not successful attempts. Existing
fixture histories instead fork the complete SQLite store and asset ledger into
owned isolated memory stores; captures, deadlines, maintained values, lifetimes
and actual media identities survive unchanged. No source store is modified.
Temporary asset directories belong to Store and disappear on drop.

Actors include all principal values in fixtures. Required/optional references
range over fixture rows, not only the first. Short histories visit first steps
breadth first before extending them under a reported work cap. Read and row
assertions are checked after each step. Write/operation assertions judge only
actual committed changes, correct insert/update/delete arms and pre-write
policy state; ambiguous multiple writes, implicit effects or unstable policy
need executed-effect tracing and remain unsupported. A finite trace is never
an induction proof for always or arbitrary histories.

Every counterexample has a machine-readable origin: authored trace from trusted
fixture, trusted-seed-only, or synthetic operation. Only the first establishes
application reachability from the recorded trusted starting state and fails
normal check/test. Seed/synthetic witnesses remain refutations of all-snapshot
claims, visible with --all and JSON, without failing acceptance. Unproved
reachability is never asserted unreachable as a mathematical theorem. Check
loads the same fixture base for reachable histories as test, without HTTP or
business-case execution. A stronger witness must replace a synthetic witness
for the same declaration. --all exposes all witnesses and never changes which
ones fail. No unreachable refutation may print pass/ok beside exit 1.

### 12.3 Actionable authority advice

N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION identifies authored caller operations that write role
or group grants trusted by later rules while the effective admission does not
establish independent authority to confer that particular grant. Include
self-signup roles and transitions where a caller can confer a role they lack.
A trusted service seat or an independently authorized administrator may appoint
other roles; holding an unrelated ordinary role does not establish delegation.
Advice names the operation, grant and gate and points to guide staging's
operator first-admin recipe. It is advice, never an implicit runtime refusal
or a proof of malicious intent. Legitimate open enrollment can be intentional.

N_GRANT_OUTLIVES_ROLE requires an actually mutable/revocable current-role
population and a retained principal/grant branch lacking that role recheck;
cover read AND write branches. Immutable/static role populations do not fire.
N_EXISTENCE_ORACLE excludes public populations and caller-owned role lookups;
only an absent/hidden distinction crossing a narrower private audience warrants
advice. Every security note has a real source location and contributes to the
reported note count. Rule printing preserves connective precedence. Identity
429 responses include a conservative retry delay covering the rate window;
the existing limits and authentication semantics are unchanged.

### 12.4 Evidence

Qualification uses read-only copies of all ten wave-17 high lanes and their
spec-authored invariant sets, plus all 150 earlier schemas. Report caught,
partial and not caught separately; all-snapshot witnesses alone do not count
as a reachable detection. Preserve bad/fixed controls, note counts and source
hashes. The open qualification-gap ticket remains open for unresolved whole
proof/device/opaque obligations. No zero-hole claim follows from finite tests.


### 12.5 SEC3 reduction details

The direct column theorem is `(row_read AND owner_private_gate) => P` in the
existing three-valued kernel; deny-delete is never an admission for column
read. Whole proof still requires every observer obligation. This increment
uses dynamic column-world observers, not an unimplemented static dataflow
claim: pure projections can avoid a refutation, but do not acquire a proof by
absence of finite counterexamples. Census is existing point/scan/view/filter/
count/sum/exists, mutation return/refusal/copy, asset, sync, raw/derived/opaque
paths. Unsupported field flow remains unproved. Audit's `column_audiences`
maps every visible column name to its effective row-and-private-field rule;
operations and derived edges remain separately enumerated.

column has two different schema-valid typed values, and policy is false on its
respective image in each committed world. Every non-native authored query and

results are declined. Compare typed logical data and authored refusal messages,
retain ordered arrays, repeat both observations to reject unstable outputs.

or recorded fields. Required media substitutions without a coupled real ledger
remain explicitly unsupported. A primary observation from the exact trusted

### 12.6 Disclosure and clock clarifications

A column observer mutation is declined if it can change the protected image,

//! Effective column payload audience; unresolved observer edges stay explicit.
use super::*;
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#121-column-read-intent — a private owner gate narrows only the column payload.
pub(super) fn admission(table:&snapback4_core::schema::Table,column:Option<&str>)->RuleExpr{
    let mut rules=vec![super::temporal::admission(table)];
    if let Some(owner)=column.and_then(|c|table.written_by.get(c)).filter(|o|!o.audience){
        rules.push(RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Field(owner.owner.clone()),RuleTerm::Viewer));
    }
    RuleExpr::And(rules)
}
//! Column worlds execute authored observers, including derived/count/refusal paths.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#121-column-read-intent — excluding a field from a projection must not produce a synthetic whole-row refutation.
use super::*;
fn alternative(kind:&C,old:&Value)->Option<Value>{Some(match (kind.required(),old){
    (C::Text{max},Value::String(s)) if s.is_empty()=>if *max>0{Value::String("x".into())}else{return None},
    (C::Text{..},Value::String(s))=>Value::String(s.chars().map(|c|if c=='y'{'x'}else{'y'}).collect()),
    (C::Enum{variants},_)=>Value::String(variants.iter().find(|s|Value::String((*s).clone())!=*old)?.clone()),
    (C::Principal,Value::Id(s))=>Value::Id(if s==VIEWER{OTHER}else{VIEWER}.into()),
    (C::Int|C::Time,Value::Int(n))=>Value::Int(n.checked_add(1)?),
    (C::IntRange{min,max},Value::Int(n))=>Value::Int(if n<max{n+1}else if n>min{n-1}else{return None}),
    (C::Bool,Value::Bool(v))=>Value::Bool(!v),
    _=>return None,
})}
fn sample_rows(bound:&Bound,rows:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,inv:&Invariant,row:&Value)->Option<BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>>{
    let column=inv.column.as_ref()?;let kind=bound.schema.tables[&inv.table].columns.get(column)?;
    let alt=alternative(kind,row.field(column).ok()?)?;
    let mut out=rows.clone();
    let selected=out.get_mut(&inv.table)?.iter_mut().find(|r|r.field("id").ok()==row.field("id").ok())?;
    if let Value::Row(fields)=selected{fields.insert(column.clone(),alt);}else{return None;}
    Some(out)
}
fn run_pair(bound:&Bound,inv:&Invariant,rows:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,row:&Value,fixture:Option<&Engine>,trace:Option<(&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,&[histories::Step])>,out:&mut Campaign){
    let Some(other)=sample_rows(bound,rows,inv,row)else{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}.{}: column pair needs a second typed non-asset value",inv.table,inv.column.as_deref().unwrap_or("")));return;};
    let altrow=other[&inv.table].iter().find(|r|r.field("id").ok()==row.field("id").ok()).unwrap();
    let mut actors=histories::viewers(bound);
    for (table,rs) in rows{for r in rs{for (c,k) in &bound.schema.tables[table].columns{if matches!(k.required(),C::Principal)&&let Ok(Value::Id(p))=r.field(c)&&!actors.contains(p){actors.push(p.clone());}}}}
    for viewer in &actors{
        if policy(bound,inv,rows,viewer,None,row)!=Ok(false)||policy(bound,inv,&other,viewer,None,altrow)!=Ok(false){continue;}
        for bp in &bound.programs{
            let p=&bp.program;
            if !matches!(p.kind,ProgramKind::Query|ProgramKind::Mutation)||p.native{continue;}
            let json=serde_json::to_string(p).unwrap();
            if json.contains("NewId")||json.contains("Emit"){continue;}
            if p.kind==ProgramKind::Mutation {
                let writes=ir::write_tables(p);
                let deps=inv.policy.as_ref().map(kernel::dependencies).unwrap_or_default();
                if writes.iter().any(|(t,_)|t==&inv.table||deps.contains(t)){
                    out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: column mutation can change protected row/policy before disclosure; boundary tracing required",p.name));continue;
                }
                if !writes.is_empty()&&(!bound.schema.maintains.is_empty()||bound.schema.tables.values().any(|t|t.on_delete.values().any(|a|a.writes()))){out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: implicit writes can change column policy before disclosure",p.name));continue;}
            }
            let args_list=histories::fixture_cases(&p.args,rows,&actors,out);
            for args in args_list{
                let execute=|alternate:bool|->Result<serde_json::Value,String>{
                    let seeds=if alternate{&other}else{rows};
                    let mut e=if let Some(fixture)=fixture{
                        let mut e=fork(bound,fixture,Vec::new())?;
                        if alternate{
                            let t=&bound.schema.tables[&inv.table];
                            let Value::Row(fields)=altrow else{return Err("not a row".into());};
                            let clean=Value::Row(fields.iter().filter(|(c,_)|!t.engine_field(c)&&!t.recorded.contains_key(*c)&&!t.maintained.contains_key(*c)).map(|(c,v)|(c.clone(),v.clone())).collect());
                            e.seed_at([(inv.table.clone(),clean)],NOW).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
                        }e
                    }else{engine(bound,seeds,program("$column.pair",ProgramKind::Query,vec![Stmt::Return(Expr::Literal(Value::Null))]))?};
                    let actual=readback(&e)?;
                    let current=actual.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten().find(|r|r.field("id").ok()==row.field("id").ok()).ok_or("column target is absent after validation")?;
                    if policy(bound,inv,&actual,viewer,None,current)!=Ok(false){return Err("validated column-world policy is not false".into());}
                    if alternate&&fixture.is_some(){
                        for (table,rs) in rows.iter().filter(|(table,_)|bound.schema.maintain_for(table).is_none()){
                            let definition=&bound.schema.tables[table];
                            let same=rs.iter().all(|old|actual.get(table).into_iter().flatten().find(|r|r.field("id").ok()==old.field("id").ok()).is_some_and(|new|definition.columns.keys().filter(|c|!definition.maintained.contains_key(*c)&&!(table==&inv.table&&inv.column.as_ref()==Some(*c)&&old.field("id").ok()==row.field("id").ok())).all(|c|old.field(c).ok()==new.field(c).ok())));
                            if !same{return Err(format!("alternate seed changes non-protected base fields in {table}; capture/recorded reduction unavailable"));}
                        }
                    }
                    paired::observe(&mut e,p,viewer,args.clone())
                };
                let (a,b)=match (execute(false),execute(true)){
                    (Ok(a),Ok(b))=>(a,b),
                    (Err(why),_)|(_,Err(why))=>{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}.{} / {}: declined column world: {why}",inv.table,inv.column.as_deref().unwrap_or(""),p.name));continue;}
                };
                out.attempted+=2;*out.operations.entry(p.name.clone()).or_default()+=2;
                if a==b{continue;}
                if !matches!((execute(false),execute(true)),(Ok(aa),Ok(bb)) if aa==a&&bb==b){continue;}
                out.violations.push(Counterexample{reachability:if fixture.is_some(){Reachability::AuthoredFixtureTrace}else{Reachability::SyntheticOperation},invariant_site:inv.site.to_string(),args:args.clone(),viewer:viewer.clone(),operation:p.name.clone(),effect:"column read observation".into(),row:row.clone(),old:Some(altrow.clone()),supporting_rows:trace.map(|(initial,_)|initial.clone()).unwrap_or_else(||rows.clone()),history:trace.map(|(_,steps)|steps.to_vec()).unwrap_or_default(),rule_path:format!("authored observer depends on {}.{} while policy is false in both typed worlds",inv.table,inv.column.as_deref().unwrap()),scope:if fixture.is_some(){"primary observation executes on trusted fixture; alternate column is a dependency test, not an authored alternate-history claim"}else{"schema-valid synthetic worlds; authored observer, no application reachability claim"}.into(),observations:Some(paired::Observations{present:a,absent:b,absent_rows:other.clone()})});
                return;
            }
        }
    }
}
pub(super) fn generated(bound:&Bound,out:&mut Campaign,sites:Option<&BTreeSet<String>>){
    for inv in bound.invariants.iter().filter(|i|i.target=="read"&&i.column.is_some()&&sites.is_none_or(|s|s.contains(&i.site.to_string()))){
        if inv.policy.as_ref().is_some_and(super::super::temporal::has_clock){out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}.{}: column clock pairs use the campaign clock only; other temporal ticks not covered",inv.table,inv.column.as_deref().unwrap()));}
        for variant in 0..16{
            let rows=match snapshot(bound,inv,variant){Ok(r)=>r,Err(why)=>{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: column snapshot: {why}",inv.table));continue;}};
            let e=match engine(bound,&rows,program("$column.seed",ProgramKind::Query,vec![Stmt::Return(Expr::Literal(Value::Null))])){Ok(e)=>e,Err(why)=>{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: column seed: {why}",inv.table));continue;}};
            let Ok(rows)=readback(&e)else{continue;};
            for row in rows.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten(){run_pair(bound,inv,&rows,row,None,None,out);}
            if out.violations.iter().any(|v|v.invariant_site==inv.site.to_string()){break;}
        }
    }
}
pub(super) fn fixture(bound:&Bound,fixture:&Engine,out:&mut Campaign){
    let Ok(rows)=readback(fixture)else{return;};
    for inv in bound.invariants.iter().filter(|i|i.target=="read"&&i.column.is_some()){
        for row in rows.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten(){run_pair(bound,inv,&rows,row,Some(fixture),None,out);}
    }
}

pub(super) fn after_history(bound:&Bound,fixture:&Engine,initial:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,steps:&[histories::Step],out:&mut Campaign){
    let rows=match readback(fixture){Ok(r)=>r,Err(why)=>{out.unsupported.insert(why);return;}};
    for inv in bound.invariants.iter().filter(|i|i.target=="read"&&i.column.is_some()){
        if out.violations.iter().any(|v|v.invariant_site==inv.site.to_string()&&v.reachability==Reachability::AuthoredFixtureTrace&&!v.history.is_empty()){continue;}
        for row in rows.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten(){run_pair(bound,inv,&rows,row,Some(fixture),Some((initial,steps)),out);}
    }
}

//! Concrete, isolated challenges; finite campaigns never establish proof.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#4-counterexamples-and-generated-tests — only replayed admission is a refutation.
use super::*;
use snapback4_core::{Engine,Store,Context,Value,RuntimeError};
use snapback4_core::schema::{ColumnType as C,Constraint};
use snapback4_core::engine::rules::{self,Probe};
use snapback4_core::ir::{self,Expr,Stmt,Program,ProgramKind,Read,KeyProvenance};
pub const NOW:i64=1_800_000_000_000;
const VIEWER:&str="dev:security-a";
const OTHER:&str="dev:security-b";
// Same verified 1x1 PNG header used by the existing packaged asset witnesses.
const PNG:[u8;24]=[137,80,78,71,13,10,26,10,0,0,0,13,73,72,68,82,0,0,0,1,0,0,0,1];
mod paired;
mod timed;
