//! Saved-source fixture selection, business cases and the query sweep.
// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000.000#9-wave-13-saved-version-proofs-day3-2026-10-16 — contemporary fixtures belong to the selected saved source.
use super::*;

const CASE_SUMMARY: &str = "SNAPBACK4_TEST_CASES";

fn case_count(path: &std::path::Path) -> Result<usize, String> {
    let text = std::fs::read_to_string(path).map_err(|error| format!("cannot read {}: {error}", path.display()))?;
    Ok(text.lines().filter(|line| {
        let line = line.trim();
        !line.is_empty() && !line.starts_with("//")
    }).count())
}

/// `test`: run every argument-free query as each persona against the store
/// (optionally loaded from a fixture) and print the meters beside the
/// ceilings, so a builder reads numbers instead of being refused on a bound.
pub fn test(options: &Options) -> i32 {
    let project = match proof_source::project(options, "source") { Ok(project) => project, Err(e) => { eprintln!("test: {e}"); return 2; } };
    let sources = match proof_source::sources(&project, options, "source") { Ok(sources) => sources, Err(e) => { eprintln!("test: {e}"); return 2; } };
    let bound = compiled_or_exit(proof_source::compile(&sources, options, "source"), options.json);
    let mut defaults = options.clone();
    let mut skipped_business_cases = 0;
    if !defaults.flags.contains_key("fixture") {
        let saved = options.flags.get("source").map(std::path::Path::new).map(|path| {
            if path.is_dir() { path.join("fixtures") }
            else if path.file_name().is_some_and(|name| name == "schema.q") { path.parent().unwrap().join("fixtures") }
            else { path.with_file_name(format!("{}-fixtures", path.file_stem().unwrap_or_default().to_string_lossy())) }
        });
        if let Some(fixture) = saved.filter(|path| path.is_dir()) {
            eprintln!("test: using {} (saved version's contemporary fixtures; override with --fixture <dir>)", fixture.display());
            defaults.flags.insert("fixture".into(), fixture.to_string_lossy().into_owned());
        } else if project.root.join("fixtures").is_dir() {
            let fixture = project.root.join("fixtures");
            if options.flags.contains_key("source") {
                defaults.flags.insert("saved-fixture-fallback".into(), String::new());
                eprintln!("test: no contemporary fixtures for saved source; validating only declared-table files in {}; pass --fixture <dir> for a complete contemporary case suite", fixture.display());
                let cases = fixture.join(test_cases::FILE);
                if cases.is_file() {
                    skipped_business_cases = match case_count(&cases) { Ok(count) => count, Err(error) => { eprintln!("test: {error}"); return 1; } };
                    eprintln!("test: skipped {} — working cases may target another release; pass --fixture <dir> to run explicitly", cases.display());
                }
            } else { eprintln!("test: using {} (default fixture directory; override with --fixture <dir>)", fixture.display()); }
            defaults.flags.insert("fixture".into(), fixture.to_string_lossy().into_owned());
        } else if options.flags.contains_key("source") {
            eprintln!("test: no saved or working fixtures; query sweep only; pass --fixture <dir> for business cases");
        }
    }
    let options = &defaults;
    // test applies dev's cast: a persona dev would refuse is not run as an ordinary viewer.
    // Residency measures any principal in the data (scale fixtures name thousands), so it notes instead.
    if let Some(why) = options.flags.get("as").and_then(|names| names.split(',').find_map(|name| test_cases::undeclared_persona(name, &bound.personas))) {
        if !options.flags.contains_key("residency") {
            eprintln!("test --as: {why}");
            return 1;
        }
        eprintln!("test --residency --as: measuring an undeclared principal; dev would refuse it as a persona");
    }
    let _owner = if options.flags.contains_key("fixture") || options.flags.contains_key("source") { None } else {
        match crate::store_owner::command(&project.store_path()) {
            Ok(owner) => Some(owner),
            Err(error) => { eprintln!("{error}"); return 1; }
        }
    };
    let fixture_now = snapback4_core::now_ms();
    let opened = match &_owner {
        Some(owner) => owner.open_engine(&bound, std::time::Duration::from_secs(5), fixture_now).map_err(|e| e.to_string()),
        None => test_fixture::engine_at(&project, &bound, options, fixture_now),
    };
    let mut engine = match opened {
        Ok(engine) => engine,
        Err(error) => { eprintln!("{error}"); return 1; }
    };
    let personas = residency::personas(&bound, options);
    let sweep_personas = if options.flags.contains_key("saved-fixture-fallback") { Default::default() } else {
        match test_sweep::load(options.flags.get("fixture"), &bound) {
            Ok(matrix) => matrix,
            Err(error) => { eprintln!("{error}"); return 1; }
        }
    };
    if let Some(persona) = options.flags.get("as") && !sweep_personas.is_empty() {
        println!("test --as {persona} overrides test.sweep.json because --as requests one deliberate global probe; for: {}", sweep_personas.keys().cloned().collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", "));
    }
    if options.flags.contains_key("residency") {
        let verbose = options.flags.contains_key("verbose");
        if let Err(error) = residency::measure(&mut engine, &bound, &personas, verbose) { eprintln!("residency measurement failed: {error}"); return 1; }
        if !verbose {
            if options.flags.contains_key("source") {
                println!("{CASE_SUMMARY} {{\"run\":0,\"skipped\":{skipped_business_cases}}}");
            }
# What Snapback 4 Does Not Do

Longer than the doing-list on purpose. Moving something off it means writing one
line naming what it unblocks and taking something off the doing-list in the same
change. Nothing comes off during slice one (LLP 3000 §3: the clock is four weeks).

## The bars

The bakeoff gates in [LLP 3000 §3](../llp/3000-snapback4.explainer.md) are the
bars: the loop bar, hub acquisition within 2× the measured floor, client bytes
under the competitor-anchored budgets, the six scenarios green in the simulator
on the hub fixture. Everything not required to pass them does not exist yet.

## Not building

- **A second authoring language.** Quarry, one spelling, copied from Snapback 2 (LLP 1004, LLP 1007 §4b). TypeScript is for seeds, journeys, effect bodies and the client.
- **Byte- and step-priced certification.** No closure, heap, output or step ceilings at `check`; the certificate certifies shape, uniformity, completeness class and prediction eligibility. Resources are metered at runtime (LLP 2000.000 §6).
- **A per-query result cache.** No range receipts, validators, halos, absence evidence, coverage spans, extent facts, held directories, fact integrity or swap journals. The client replicates declared partitions with one watermark each (LLP 2000.000 §7).
- **Engine-minted ids for client inserts, grants, ID slices.** Ids are client-minted, time-ordered, 128-bit; the engine mints for its own writes.
- **The `drain` protocol, follower-tab inbox rows, `defineItems`, `withHistory`, `mutationTarget`, `receipt.source`, `local.caughtUp`, `partial` reason codes.** The card (`packages/snapback4/src/contract.ts`) is the whole client vocabulary; `why` carries the rest.
- **`retain` as a receipt rule.** `until-revoked` and `delivered-history` survive as the client's leave policy on a scope tombstone.
- **JavaScript in the data plane.** Queries and mutations run in the Rust interpreter inside the SQLite transaction, and in the same interpreter on the client (host decided at week 3 by measurement, LLP 2000.000 §9). A *job* may be the app's own Rust, hosted as wasm through the interpreter's operations under the job's meter (LLP 3000.005, the Moves line below); a query or mutation may not.
- **An embedded JS engine for effects.** The data plane never calls out. `emit` writes the effect durably in the mutation's commit and an external worker holding a service token claims, performs and settles it over the wire (`snapback4/worker`); the four reference effects run on the builder's Node (LLP 2000.000 §10, decided 2026-09-13).
- **Committed generated code; an auth mode; a supervised bundler.** As Snapback 2 (`rules/NOT-DOING.md`): types by inference, personas not modes, `SNAPBACK_URL` is the client contract.
- **A TypeScript twin of the interpreter.** Retired by the author's ruling of 2026-09-13 (LLP 3000 §7): one implementation, in Rust, on every host — the core's SQLite under the native module, memory under wasm on the web with the host persisting each commit's changes. The web pays 312,924 B gzip before the diet where the twin's tier paid 16,842; the conformance obligation between two interpreters ends. Expo Go runs the online tier.
- **More than one hosted target, an admin console, media transcoding or thumbnails, push delivery itself (a worker's), cron syntax beyond `every N at HH:MM`, vector or relevance-ranked search (word-prefix search is in: `words(..)` and `matching`), federation, multi-region, device-to-device sync, at-rest encryption claims.** After the clock, on evidence.
- **General observability beyond `why`, `trace`, `logs`, `data`, `sql`, server operation latency, and bounded client subscription inspection.** The `sql` verb is read-only inspection of the developer's own store (LLP 3000.007 §8.8), never an application surface. Separately declared application SQL reads are admitted for increment D (LLP 3000.009 §6.2, §14.4; the 2026-09-16 Moves line below), not implemented by this inspection verb.
- **A guide corpus.** The compiler's facts, one language card and keyword cards, as Snapback 2; no freshness dates. Every card's Quarry is compiled by the suite and the language card is run, so a card cannot promise a form the parser refuses.

- **Partial union delivery, two group audiences, a public branch.** A union syncs principal-column branches and at most one group branch, each a whole disjunct of the read rule (LLP 3000.036.000.000); a branch with an extra condition, a second group or a `public` branch is refused with the rewrite, never partially delivered; service grants add no device audience (LLP 3000.007 §20.6). A union does not combine with delivered history, read-through children, synced or row-keyed views, owner keys, timelines, private field owners, acquisition windows or direct/inherited expiry on content or membership scopes.
- **Exact horizon maintenance.** The horizon bounds acquisition (a snapshot, a bootstrap); the log never evicts or back-fills to hold a device's group at exactly N rows. A local read past the acquired horizon by key says `complete: false`.

- **Clock expressions in rules.** A read rule compares with `=` and `!=` only; `.expiresAt > now` remains a mutation `require`. Stored lifetimes use `not before .col` for server-controlled opening and `expire at .col` for expiry. The engine publishes opening commits; devices receive no unopened payload. The supported compositions and explicit exclusions are in LLP 3000.036.003.
- **A jobs framework.** No priorities, worker pools or dead letters: one FIFO of runs, one chunk at a time, a refused chunk parked with its refusal. Actions, effects and scheduled actions carry per-operation retries, deadlines and cancellation, and a record per external step (LLP 3000.009 §5.2; the Moves line below). Missed period boundaries while a server is down fire once on return, not once per missed period.
- **Asset processing.** No transcoding, thumbnails, rungs or HEIC: the bytes are served as uploaded; JPEG, PNG, WebP and MP4 only; image dimensions and optional MP4 duration read from the header; MP4 dimensions are not extracted.
- **Search beyond word prefixes.** One tokenizer (NFC, lowercase, alphanumeric words), one index shape (`scope, words(cols), order.., id`), one read (`matching`): every term a prefix of some word, the first term's words merged as lanes, bounded by the meter in a uniform scope, at most 32 words in an admitted non-uniform scope. No stemming, no ranking by relevance, no phrase or mid-word matching, no per-language folding, no intersection index for the second term (it filters the page).
- **An identity provider.** Guests, password accounts, service seats, and sessions a service mints for accounts the application authenticated itself. No phone, OAuth, magic links or MFA in the engine: an application's worker verifies those and mints the session.
- **No multi-server presence, no presence history or last-seen storage, no broadcast event stream, no connection-bound rows.** `connected[p]` stays server-wide, counting held polls and their 35 s grace; a live query that may read it re-runs in the background about every 5 seconds to stay current (LLP 3000.018). Typing and viewing use short-lived, authorized ephemeral tables in one server process (LLP 3000.017); they never sync or decide durable invariants. A device does not know presence and says so.

## Process not doing

- **2026-09-28 — ordinary mixed search** (LLP 3000.028 §3.3, lane OS round 1).
  Admit the SDK's bounded merge of current server rows and certified Left images
  for the proved single time-ordered word page. This removes application-owned
  online/held selection for that shape. One server call per requested page;
  recomputation pays again from consumed positions. The coordinator shares the
  existing device read allowance. No durable search session, extra synced rows,
  new authority or additional allowance is admitted.

- **2026-09-28 — inferred current-scope word search** (LLP 3000.028 §3.3,
  lane OS). A single bare ordinary word page may derive the exact non-expiring
  membership audience for the existing compact server plan. This removes the
  requirement to repeat that audience predicate in this narrow server query.
  The compiler supplies a scoped words index when absent; it is an ordinary
  schema index, maintained on writes and on devices, with no extra synced rows
  or new authority. The device keeps its original bare scan. Round 0 left ordered composition
  and combined accounting outside its independent subset; round 1 above admits
  that bounded SDK composition. Durable search sessions remain excluded.
- **2026-10-12 — union sync audiences** (LLP 3000.036.000.000, workstream UNION, two blind review rounds). A table syncs to the union of principal-column audiences and one group audience; a device holds a row while any branch admits its viewer. What it unblocks: owner-or-team, buyer-or-seller and owner-or-staff tables offline for every reader, the shape three app farms asked for. Taken off: "one `sync` audience per table". Partial delivery, two groups and a public branch stay excluded (above).
- **2026-09-28 — explicit owner-key coverage** (LLP 3000.028 §3.4, Lane OK commission). A public window may opt into a compiler-owned, horizon-free principal key partition to answer ordinary own-key existence offline. Taken off: the exclusion of narrower platform key distribution beside one public window. General union audiences, full own-row replication and per-query absence caches remain excluded. The projection uses existing metered maintains, adoption and whole-partition publication; existing windows do not expand.
- **2026-09-26 — a live query keeps presence current** (LLP 3000.018). A 0.4.2 probe found an observed `connected[p]` reporting `fresh: true` while stale for over a minute, and a comparative builder polled around it. What it unblocks: online marks and presence-aware lists that stay current. Taken off: "a presence stream" as an exclusion for live queries; a presence API, last-seen storage and multi-server presence stay out. Under the author's standing authorization for reworks that make queries excellent (2026-09-25).

- **No drift registries, inventories, line-pinned diagnostics registries, claim digests or frozen-record gates.** Generated projections are built, not committed.
- **No gate bound to an exact commit; no push gated on a battery.** Five blocking checks in sixty seconds, run before push; everything slower on a schedule with bounded tickets.
- **No ceiling raised in slice one.** Budgets are anchored to a competitor's measured number and recorded once in the journal.
- **No standing panel** except the two blind reviews LLP 3000 decision 4 names (partition inversion; the meter's cut on non-uniform scans). The author explicitly commissioned the one-off LLP 3000.008 panel and disjoint Astra implementation lanes on 2026-09-14; its bounded disposition is recorded in that umbrella and LLP 3000 §7.
- **No new LLP number** without first extending LLP 3000 or LLP 2000.000; no spec without running code (`rules/RULES.md`: only implemented specs bind).
- **No second session on the core** during slice one; lanes take the periphery.
- **No test matrix as the primary harness.** The simulator is; real-browser and device witnesses cover only what it cannot reach.
- **No files over 1,500 lines**, including ones copied from Snapback 2. Split on
  the way in. The one exception is the package's published documents
  (`packages/snapback4/README.md` and its linked references): the rule exists to
  keep a *source* file reviewable, and the 0.2.25 calibration measured the cost of
  applying it to a document — splitting the README was that run's single largest
  scoring loss, because the builder then had four places to look instead of one
  (LLP 3000.007 §20.19). A published document is bounded by whether a builder can
  find one way in, not by a line count; the census that enforces this rule covers
  source, and says so.

## Deliberately worse

- No compatibility with Snapback 1 or Snapback 2 apps, packages or on-disk formats.
- No API stability before the clock; every publish is `0.0.x`.
- Sparse prose documentation; the diagnostics and the recipes carry the load.
- One hosted target, SQLite in the production CLI, one platform binary at a time. A separately enabled Postgres evaluation adapter is not a hosted/deployable target (Moves below).

## Moves

- **2026-10-13 — stored lower lifetimes (`not before`) are commissioned by PAGE2** (LLP 3000.036.003). This unblocks timed disclosure without an application publication job. Per-application jobs that flip a publication flag leave this admitted path; clock expressions in rules, captured lower lifetimes for read-through children, and boundary recounts for maintained data remain excluded. HS owns handshake transport; PAGE2 declares the new semantic capability.

- **2026-10-06 — exact Following history (FH delegated round-1 ruling).**
  Admit a Following-specific server traversal and bounded volatile frontier/replay
  state under LLP 3000.026 §9. The approved design reads exact history online
  from the first page; the SDK will own continuation. The first milestone is internal event metadata
  and the oracle, with no public completeness claim. Taken off: a blanket ban on
  state for this one expiring read. General result caches, resumable queries,
  device traversal, inbox coverage certificates and history-only blocks remain
  excluded. Existing inbox bytes and runtime ceilings remain unchanged.

- **2026-09-26 — server operation latency (LLP 3000.019 §3.7).** Calibration #22
  measured each read once and could not claim repeated p95/degradation testing.
  Unblocks percentile measurements of the application's own load without a
  custom timing harness. Taken off: a latency harness written by the builder;
  general metrics, tracing infrastructure and an admin console remain excluded.

- **2026-09-23 — Quarry states intent moves onto the doing-list** (Charlie:
  "if you need to try making *significant* architectural reworks in order to
  make the data modeling, queries, and scalability be excellent and elegant,
  you can experiment with that"; LLP 3000.013). Unblocks reads that state
  predicates and order, one `view` declaration for derived data, Following
  without caps, jobs and aggregates without author-chosen bounds, an app
  object and an offline web shell. Taken off: index names and author-chosen
  engine numbers on the paved path; the timeline's promotion, pull routes and
  caps; the "Time in rules" refusal is unchanged. Stands: one interpreter, no
  JavaScript in the data plane, no lowering Quarry to SQL, no relevance-ranked
  search.

- **2026-09-16 — the remaining general-purpose program moves onto the doing-list**
  (Charlie: "complete everything ... we'll remove services when we are done
  with the workroom test rather than wait to add them in ... use the fleet";
  LLP 3000.009 §14.7). Unblocks the §7 integrated-service baseline, C3 production
  Postgres, D's authorized database capabilities and measurement-led C2/E
  work while Workroom continues. Taken off: waiting for Workroom before
  starting services, and keeping production Postgres integration outside the
  current implementation program. The completed Workroom record drives a
  retain/remove pass. Stands: one interpreter and managed writer, no managed
  SQL writes, no JavaScript transactions, no SQL in the first Workroom cell,
  no invented performance/real-provider evidence, no expo-build-1000 load,
  and no new publication or managed-service deployment authority.

- **2026-09-16 — application SQL reads move onto the doing-list** (Charlie: "admit application SQL reads"; LLP 3000.009 §6.2, §14.4, increment D). Unblocks reporting and search over authorized projection inputs and declared database-owned tables, with authorization before selection, ordering and limits. Taken off in the same change: the inspection-only exclusion for application SQL and D's pending-author gate. Implementation and qualification remain pending; SQLite and Postgres halves ship together, channels follow candidates, and A1–A3 plus the fixture pack stay first. Stands: no SQL writes into managed data, no lowering Quarry to SQL, no SQL in the first Snapback brief cell, and no publication authorization.

- **2026-09-15 — a Postgres evaluation backend moves onto the doing-list** (author: "Build the evaluation prototype"). Unblocks concrete evaluation of storage agnosticism, interpreter/database round trips and transactional sync ordering. This bounded prototype replaces full CLI/deployment integration as the next backend task; those product surfaces remain off this increment's doing-list. It shares Quarry, rules, maintains and partition logic, but uses an experimental Postgres store. Native SQL exists only as an explicitly separate comparison workload, not an application escape hatch. SQLite remains the production and local-device store. See [the experiment](./experiments/postgres/README.md).

- **2026-09-15 — the server half of LLP 3000.009 moves onto the doing-list** (author: "begin implementation using astra xhigh to code"; LLP 3000.009 §10.2, increments A1–A3). Unblocks the online-only brief's invitation, webhook and scheduled journeys without a second application server: work records whose authority is bound at admission, ids bound to their operation and arguments, declared action results, a generated handler context with a durable record per external step, declared routes and webhooks, scheduled actions under service seats, and `serve` supervising the runtime. Taken off in the same change: "A jobs framework" narrows — actions, effects and scheduled actions get per-operation retries, deadlines and cancellation; priorities, worker pools and dead letters stay out — and the actions card's "supervise the worker as an ordinary Node process" leaves. Stands: no JavaScript in the data plane, no identity provider in the engine, and no `ctx.sql`, projections or SQL reads in this increment.

- **2026-09-15 — the Postgres access boundary moves onto the doing-list** (LLP 3000.009 §10.2, increment C1). Unblocks measuring Postgres behind a batched access boundary instead of row-at-a-time calls: transaction-scoped prefetch, a pipelined commit tail, one portable index-key bound checked on both stores, and the corpus, conformance and device suites on both stores. Taken off in the same change: the evaluation prototype's single-adapter framing, in which the only next step was an unbatched store. Stands: Postgres is not a hosted or deployable target; its refusals of assets, jobs, actions and schedules, and `serve`, `deploy` and backup on Postgres, wait for increment C3.

- **2026-09-13 — word search, effects, service seats and presence move onto the doing-list** (author: "add all the things we need for Clique and Interview"; the full-brief Clique record's remaining capability lines and Interview's adapter responsibilities). What it unblocks: Clique M5's three search scopes online and offline on one index, M6's push seam without connection leases hand-built in tables, M8's digest emitted rather than logged into a table, Interview's phone sign-in and model workers as a service that mints sessions instead of proxying every request, and the ranked feed of LLP 3000.004 on the `feeds` card. Taken off in the same change: "an embedded JS engine for effects, until measured" (the engine never calls out; a worker does), "word search" from the package's exclusions. Stands: no JavaScript in the data plane, no relevance ranking, no identity provider in the engine.
- **2026-09-12 — expiry, assets and jobs move onto the doing-list** (author: "complete snapback4 to the point where the entire clique benchmark can be run"). What it unblocks: the struck half of the Clique brief (M4 media, M5 view-once and expiry with no client open, M8's scheduled digest). Taken off in the same change: the "one photo path" and "cron syntax" exclusions above narrow to transcoding and to cron beyond `every N [at HH:MM]`; the "time in rules" exclusion stands, answered by `expire at .col` rather than a rule.
- **2026-09-13 — native jobs on (author: "write up an LLP about this and then implement").** Unblocks the scoring half of a feed — floats, vectors, real loops, a typed schema — without putting any of it into Quarry, and gives the model half of LLP 3000.004 a seat inside the bounds: one chunk per commit, the job ceilings, fuel, no I/O. Off the doing-list in the same change: the "external ranker with a service seat" of LLP 3000.004 §6.5 is no longer the only shape for compute over the store; it remains the shape for the network. Ranked and vector search, effects execution and an embedded JS engine stay above.
- **2026-09-13 — `sql`, a fifth named observability verb, moves onto the doing-list** (author ruling on LLP 3000.007 §14.10). Read-only SQL over generated per-table views of the developer's own store, under `dev` or against a `--release` directory; never rule-filtered, never an application surface. What it unblocks: the ground truth two Clique essays said Snapback lacked beside Postgres ("there is no underlying Postgres to query"), and the inspection the 0.2.6 Astra lane did by copying its SQLite file. Taken off in the same change: SQL reads as an application query surface (LLP 3000.007 §9.1) leave the doing-list until the reporting gap is measured after the language relaxations; reporting is Quarry's, inspection is `sql`'s. Stands: no JavaScript in the data plane, one authoring language.

- **2026-09-14 — the simulator witness is published for app builders (LLP 3000.007 §20.10, S30).** Unblocks native offline proof artifacts; replaces the repository-only Metro witness on the doing-list. Real-browser and device witnesses still cover only what the deterministic simulator cannot reach.

- **2026-09-14 — development clock control (S27, calibration F5)** moves onto the doing-list to unblock expiry and scheduled-digest tests without real-minute waits or backdated seeds; waiting on real time leaves the development test path, while production clock controls stay off the doing-list.

- **2026-09-25 — ephemeral tables enact the standing realtime line of LLP 3000.009 §12 (LLP 3000.017).** Unblocks typing and viewing without durable outbox traffic. Nothing leaves the doing-list: this enacts an already admitted scope. Last-seen storage, multi-server presence, a separate presence stream and ephemeral authority over durable invariants remain excluded.

- **2026-09-30 — bounded client subscription inspection (author's PR round-0 task)**
  moves onto the doing-list: query-group queue/in-flight/withdrawal counts and
  bounded latency/cause samples explain mounted screens flashing empty. Taken off
  the doing-list: builders must reconstruct this pressure from ad-hoc console
  instrumentation. General observability beyond the named verbs remains excluded;
  there is no telemetry transport or server admin console (LLP 3000.023 §10).
# Rules

**Snapback 2 optimizes the edit-to-verified loop — the agent's building an app on it, and ours building it.**

Speed is the constraint. Everything below exists to keep the edit-to-verified loop short
and to stop this repository accreting apparatus faster than anyone can remove it. These
rules bind `snapback2/` and the LLPs numbered 1000 and up; Snapback 1 is baselined, not
exempt, and its numbers may only fall.

Each rule is **[check]** (enforced by `caps`) or **[review]** (enforced by a human).

## Budgets — every budget is a trade, not a limit

- **5 blocking checks, 60s total.** A sixth requires deleting one, in the same PR. **[check]**
- **59 documents in the working set** (`llp/current/`). Today's count. A 60th requires
  archiving one — which is just removing the link. Snapback 2 aims to leave this at 15;
  Snapback 1's links are archived at promotion. **[check]**
- **12 documents in the foundation** (`llp/foundation/`). **[check]**
- **This file: 700 words.** If it grows, something becomes a check or stops being a rule. **[check]**
- **1,500 lines per source file, baseline 204.** Generated files exempt — and generated
  files are built, not committed; so are benchmark run records under `benchmarks/runs/`,
  which are evidence, not source. The baseline is Snapback 1's oversize count on the day
  the rule landed; it may not grow, and `snapback2/` contributes zero to it. **[check]**

## Blocking checks

`caps` · `ref-check` · `snapback2/scripts/test.sh` · `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs --check` · `check-inventories`

## Loop shape

- **Every check reports all failures in one run.** Fail-fast is banned. **[check]**
- **Nothing blocks on anything slower than 60 seconds.** Slow verification runs on a
  schedule, attributed to the breaking commit, and files a bounded set of issues. **[review]**
- **Fix loops get 3 rounds.** Then stop: escalate to a human or descope. **[review]**

## Time budgets

Tracked every run of `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs` (appends `snapback2/metrics/loop.jsonl`).
A regression is a P0 with a name on it.

| | budget |
|---|---|
| Incremental rebuild after a one-line edit | 15s |
| Test what you just changed | 30s |
| Blocking gate suite | 60s |
| Full build, warm cache | 5 min |
| **`snapback2 check` on a program (the agent's product latency)** | **1s** |
| **`snapback2 doctor` boot-to-ready** | **0.5s** |

## Scope

- **`NOT-DOING.md` is binding.** Moving something onto the doing-list means writing why
  and taking something off. **[review]**
- **Delete; don't deprecate.** No compat shims or migration paths before promotion. **[review]**
- **Nothing is compiled, transpiled, or generated at runtime that could be built ahead of
  time.** **[review]**
- **A spec needs an implementer and a date, or it isn't written.** **[review]**
- **Only implemented specs bind.** A document with no running code behind it is a
  proposal, and a proposal cannot block a PR or justify a check. **[review]**

## Agents

- **Agents remove apparatus freely and add none.** An agent PR that adds a check, script,
  registry, config file, or design doc needs explicit human approval saying so. **[review]**
- **2026-09-06 session exception:** the user delegated the search implementation
  decisions; one `snapback2-core/build.rs` may generate pinned Unicode tables at
  build time, alongside the existing package build. No new gate, registry,
  runtime generator, or Unicode dependency is authorized by this exception. **[review]**
- **Snapback 2 LLPs are numbered 1000 and up**; `llp-create` must be told. **[review]**
- **Snapback 4 LLPs are numbered 3000 and up** (`snapback4/`, LLP 3000); it adopts these budgets
  and loop shape as its own and keeps its scope in `snapback4/NOT-DOING.md`. **[review]**

## Shared machines

- Warm, named lanes, one per stream of work. Never clone fresh; reuse the worktree.
- **Never `git stash`.** Use a temporary commit.
- Kill only PIDs you recorded at launch. Never by name or pattern.

snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/schema/time_audience.rs:33:    pub fn live(&self, row: &Value, now: i64) -> bool {
//! Frozen compound traversal from 59fb4ead1b, before update image attribution.
//! Leaves and timeline algebra were not changed by RULES3.
use super::*;
pub(crate) fn evaluate(rule: &RuleExpr, viewer: &str, old: Option<&Value>, next: &Value, probe: &mut dyn Probe) -> Result<bool, crate::RuntimeError> {
    let prior=probe.replace_expiry(None);
    let result=timeline(rule,viewer,old,next,false,probe);
    probe.replace_expiry(match &result {
        Ok(answer) if answer.value==Verdict::Readable => earliest(prior,answer.changes.first().map(|(t,_)|*t)),
        _ => prior,
    });
    result.map(|answer|answer.value==Verdict::Readable)
}
fn timeline(rule: &RuleExpr, viewer: &str, old: Option<&Value>, next: &Value, created_allowed: bool, probe: &mut dyn Probe) -> Result<Timeline, crate::error::RuntimeError> {
    match rule {
        RuleExpr::And(terms) | RuleExpr::Or(terms) => {
            let conjunction = matches!(rule, RuleExpr::And(_));
            let decisive = if conjunction { Verdict::Denied } else { Verdict::Readable };
            let mut answer = Timeline::constant(decisive.not());
            for term in terms {
                answer = answer.combine(timeline(term, viewer, old, next, created_allowed, probe)?, conjunction);
                // Whole-rule expiry needs the remaining alternatives unless
                // structure/verdicts alone prove them irrelevant. An exists
                // with a permanent witness visits exactly the same branches
                // as one with a finite witness, under the same physical bound.
                // Logical guard narrowing does not assume this traversal skips
                // an operand: ordinary unique null points return a known miss
                // before touching storage (LLP 3000.013 §2.2).
                if answer.value == decisive && answer.invariant { break; }
            }
            Ok(answer)
        }
        RuleExpr::Not(inner) => {
            let mut answer = timeline(inner, viewer, old, next, created_allowed, probe)?;
            answer.value = answer.value.not();
            for (_, value) in &mut answer.changes { *value = value.not(); }
            Ok(answer)
        }
        _ => {
            probe.take_changes();
            let mut missing = false;
            let value = evaluate_leaf(rule, viewer, old, next, created_allowed, probe, &mut missing)?;
            let expiry = probe.replace_expiry(None);
            let mut answer = Timeline::constant(if probe.take_unknown() || missing { Verdict::Unknown } else if value { Verdict::Readable } else { Verdict::Denied });
            if let Some(changes) = probe.take_changes() {
                answer.changes = changes;
                answer.invariant = false;
                return Ok(answer);
            }
            // An absent witness stays absent as rows expire. Positive probes
            // (including created facts with extra tests) need their alternatives
            // in both lifetime worlds; the observed deadline cannot decide this.
            answer.invariant = rule_probes(rule) == 0 || answer.value == Verdict::Denied;
            if answer.value == Verdict::Readable && let Some(time) = expiry { answer.changes.push((time, Verdict::Denied)); }
            Ok(answer)
        }
    }
}
//! Referential actions on delete (LLP 3000.039 §3): before a delete removes
//! its row, reserve a schema-fixed charge, close over the `cascade`
//! references, refuse while a `restrict` reference still holds a closure row
//! from outside the closure, clear `set null` references outside it, and
//! remove the closure children first. The caller then removes the row itself.
// @ref LLP 3000.039#3 — closure first; probes exclude the closure; deletion dominates set null.
use crate::engine::runner::Runner;
use crate::{
    error::{MeterKind, RuntimeError},
    meter::{Ceilings, Cut, Meter},
    rows::{Bounds, Dir},
    schema::{Effect, references::{OnDelete, Referencing}},
    value::Value,
};
use std::collections::BTreeSet;

impl Runner<'_> {
    pub(super) fn apply_reference_actions(&mut self, table_name: &str, old: &Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.reference_actions(table_name, old, false)
    }

    // Recovery has no later root-delete hook to preflight physical read-through
    // retention. Include that root before the first child action can write.
    pub(in crate::engine) fn apply_missing_reference_actions(&mut self, table_name: &str, identity: &Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.reference_actions(table_name, identity, true)
    }

    fn reference_actions(&mut self, table_name: &str, old: &Value, missing_root: bool) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let schema = self.schema;
        if !schema.is_referenced(table_name) { return Ok(()); }
        let Some(price) = schema.delete_reservation(table_name) else {
            // A device without a reached child's exported price cannot charge
            // what the server charges; the server always has every price.
            if self.device { return Err(RuntimeError::Prediction(format!("deleting {table_name} needs every referencing table's write price; queued without a local delete"))); }
            return Err(RuntimeError::Schema(format!("{table_name}'s delete actions have no reservation: a cascading reference lacks its cap or its child's write price (guide writes)")));
        };
        // @ref LLP 3000.039#4.2 — what the commit publishes is fixed by the schema, not by the rows the actions reach.
        let closure = schema.action_tables(table_name);
        // A public device projection carries the private column's price but
        // not the maintain that computes its value. If an action can write
        // such a source, settlement must supply the authoritative mirror.
        // @ref LLP 3000.007.001#3.7 — devices store maintained columns but never derive them.
        if self.device && closure.written.iter().any(|source| schema.write_reservation(source)
            .is_some_and(|plan| plan.price(crate::schema::reservations::COLUMNS)[3] != 0)) {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Prediction(format!("deleting {table_name} can change a server-maintained column; queued without a local delete")));
        }
        for table in &closure.written {
            if schema.visible(table) { self.written_tables.insert(table.clone()); }
            self.action_tables.insert(table.clone());
        }
        for table in &closure.read { self.read_tables.insert(table.clone()); }
        if !closure.written.is_empty() { self.tx.widen_change_evidence(); }
        for (kind, amount) in [(MeterKind::Steps, price[0]), (MeterKind::IndexEntries, price[1]), (MeterKind::Examined, price[2]), (MeterKind::RowsWritten, price[3]), (MeterKind::OutputBytes, price[5]), (MeterKind::RuleProbes, price[6])] {
            if amount > 0 { self.meter.charge(kind, amount)?; }
        }
        // Pending event images survive the closure and subsequent statements.
        // Keep their schema-fixed reservation until the request ends.
        let events = schema.delete_event_heap(table_name).unwrap_or(0);
        self.meter.heap(events)?;
        let mark = self.meter.mark();
        self.meter.heap(price[4].saturating_sub(events))?;
        // @ref LLP 3000.039#3 — draw the reservation's rows from the request's physical allowance before the first seek.
        if !self.context.is_system() { self.tx.draw(table_name, price[2])?; }
        let meter = std::mem::replace(&mut self.meter, Meter::new(Ceilings::unbounded(), Cut::Abort));
        // The fixed action reservation prepays the closure itself, but held-
        // group and authority proofs remain device-only bounded work. Keep the
        // active allowance reachable by nested `device_work` calls while the
        // closure runs under its prepaid unbounded meter. Recursive cascade
        // removals see the already-stored allowance and must not take it over.
        // @ref LLP 3000.039#6.4 — every device-only proof spends the separate bounded physical-work allowance.
        let owns_device_allowance = self.device_work_active && self.device_allowance.is_none();
        let mut meter = Some(meter);
        if owns_device_allowance { self.device_allowance = meter.take(); }
        let prepaid = self.tx.reference_prepaid(true);
        let action = std::mem::replace(&mut self.engine_action, true);
        let result = self.reference_closure(table_name, old, missing_root);
        self.engine_action = action;
        self.tx.reference_prepaid(prepaid);
        self.meter = if owns_device_allowance {
            self.device_allowance.take().expect("active device allowance")
        } else {
            meter.expect("prepaid action meter")
        };
        self.meter.release_heap(mark);
        result
    }

    /// Steps 3–6. The walk holds identities, never images: each child scan
    /// streams `PAGE` rows per seek, and every write rereads its row current.
    fn reference_closure(&mut self, root_table: &str, root: &Value, missing_root: bool) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let schema = self.schema;
        let mut seen: BTreeSet<(String, String)> = BTreeSet::from([(root_table.to_owned(), root.field("id")?.as_str()?.to_owned())]);
        // (Vectors, not maps: the web device pays for every new collection type.)
        let mut visits: Vec<(String, Value)> = vec![(root_table.to_owned(), root.field("id")?.clone())];
        // 3. Close over cascades, breadth first. Every reached row's referencing groups must be held on a device.
        let mut next = 0;
        while next < visits.len() {
            let (table, id) = visits[next].clone();
            next += 1;
            for r in schema.referencing(&table) {
                if self.device && !through_reference(&r) { self.prove_reference_held(&r, &id)?; }
                if r.action != OnDelete::Cascade { continue; }
                self.each_child(&r, &id, true, &mut |_, child| {
                    if seen.insert((r.child.to_owned(), child.as_str()?.to_owned())) { visits.push((r.child.to_owned(), child)); }
                    Ok(true)
                })?;
            }
        }
        // 4. Restrict: every probe runs, streaming past closure rows (the root
        // included) to the first outside witness; the first occupied reference
        // in schema order is reported. One reference's probes visit each closure
        // row at most once, plus one witness per probe (LLP 3000.039 §5.1).
        let mut occupied: Option<(String, String, String)> = None;
        for (table, id) in &visits {
            for r in schema.referencing(table) {
                if r.action != OnDelete::Restrict || through_reference(&r) { continue; }
                let mut outside = false;
                self.each_child(&r, id, false, &mut |_, child| {
                    outside = !seen.contains(&(r.child.to_owned(), child.as_str()?.to_owned()));
                    Ok(!outside)
                })?;
                let candidate = (r.child.to_owned(), r.column.to_owned(), table.clone());
                if outside && occupied.as_ref().is_none_or(|o| candidate < *o) { occupied = Some(candidate); }
            }
        }
        if let Some((child, column, parent)) = occupied {
            #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
            self.refusing_clause(&child, &format!("{column}: {parent} (on delete restrict)"));
            let via = if parent == root_table { String::new() } else { format!(", through a {parent} row this delete cascades to") };
            return Err(RuntimeError::Structural { code: "E_REFERENCED", message: format!("{root_table} at {} is still referenced through {child}.{column} (on delete restrict){via}; delete or retarget those rows first, or declare the reference's action (guide writes)", self.statement_site) });
        }
        // A cascaded row can itself have a physically retained read-through
        // child. Decide that blocker before set-null writes begin, so expiry
        // can retain this root and continue without relying on transaction
        // rollback to undo a partial closure.
        for (table, id) in visits.iter().skip(if missing_root { 0 } else { 1 }) {
            if self.physical_children_outside(table, id, &seen)? {
                return Err(self.parent_has_children(table));
            }
        }
        // 5. Set null outside the closure. Each group is gathered whole before
        // any of it is cleared: a clear can recount a sibling that shares the
        // reference's index and move it behind a page cursor. Each row is then
        // reread current before its write.
        // @ref LLP 3000.039#3 — gather, then clear: no row moves out of a walk it has not finished.
        for (table, id) in &visits {
            for r in schema.referencing(table) {
                if r.action != OnDelete::SetNull { continue; }
                let mut group: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
                self.each_child(&r, id, true, &mut |_, child| {
                    let child = child.as_str()?.to_owned();
                    if !seen.contains(&(r.child.to_owned(), child.clone())) { group.push(child); }
                    Ok(true)
                })?;
                for child in group {
//! Closed enum census: a new IR statement/expression/builtin forces review.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#3-sound-proof-boundary — opaque context changes are blockers.
use snapback4_core::ir::{Expr,Stmt,Builtin};
pub(super) fn body(xs:&[Stmt])->bool{xs.iter().all(|s|match s{
    Stmt::Const{value,..}|Stmt::Let{value,..}|Stmt::Assign{value,..}|Stmt::Push{value,..}|Stmt::Return(value)=>expr(value),
    Stmt::If{condition,then_body,else_body}=>expr(condition)&&body(then_body)&&body(else_body),
    Stmt::For{..}=>false,
    Stmt::Require{condition,..}|Stmt::Check{condition,..}=>expr(condition),
    Stmt::Insert{row,..}|Stmt::InsertIfAbsent{row,..}|Stmt::Upsert{row,..}=>expr(row),
    Stmt::Update{key,patch,..}=>expr(key)&&expr(patch),
    Stmt::WriteTarget{patch,condition,..}=>expr(patch)&&condition.as_ref().is_none_or(|c|body(&c.setup)&&expr(&c.value)),
    Stmt::Delete{key,..}=>expr(key),Stmt::DeleteTarget{..}|Stmt::Break|Stmt::Continue=>true,
    Stmt::Emit{..}=>false,
})}
fn expr(e:&Expr)->bool{match e{
    Expr::Literal(_)|Expr::Variable(_)|Expr::Input(_)=>true,
    Expr::Field{base,..}|Expr::Unary{value:base,..}=>expr(base),
    Expr::Array(xs)=>xs.iter().all(expr),Expr::Object(xs)=>xs.values().all(expr),
    Expr::Shape{base,fields}=>expr(base)&&fields.values().all(expr),
    Expr::Binary{left,right,..}=>expr(left)&&expr(right),
    Expr::Block{body:xs,value}=>body(xs)&&expr(value),
    Expr::Get(r)|Expr::Exists(r)=>r.key.iter().all(expr),
    Expr::Scan(_)|Expr::Merge(_)=>false,
    Expr::Builtin{builtin,args}=>pure(builtin)&&args.iter().all(expr),
}}
fn pure(b:&Builtin)->bool{match b{
    Builtin::JobAs|Builtin::JobQuery{..}=>false,
    Builtin::ProjectFields{..}|Builtin::ReceiptSettled{..}|Builtin::OptionalField{..}|Builtin::Calendar{..}|Builtin::Civil(_)|Builtin::Len|Builtin::Slice|Builtin::Concat|Builtin::Lower|Builtin::Trim|Builtin::Sha256|Builtin::Join|Builtin::Group{..}|Builtin::First|Builtin::Last|Builtin::At|Builtin::Min|Builtin::Max|Builtin::Extremum{..}|Builtin::ExtremumOf{..}|Builtin::Divmod{..}|Builtin::Divide{..}|Builtin::Sort{..}|Builtin::MentionTokens|Builtin::SearchPresent|Builtin::RequireUncapped|Builtin::DeterministicShare|Builtin::Count|Builtin::Sum|Builtin::CountCapped|Builtin::Connected=>true,
}}
    14	fn sample_rows(bound:&Bound,rows:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,inv:&Invariant,row:&Value)->Option<BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>>{
    15	    let column=inv.column.as_ref()?;let kind=bound.schema.tables[&inv.table].columns.get(column)?;
    16	    let alt=alternative(kind,row.field(column).ok()?)?;
    17	    let mut out=rows.clone();
    18	    let selected=out.get_mut(&inv.table)?.iter_mut().find(|r|r.field("id").ok()==row.field("id").ok())?;
    19	    if let Value::Row(fields)=selected{fields.insert(column.clone(),alt);}else{return None;}
    20	    Some(out)
    21	}
    22	fn run_pair(bound:&Bound,inv:&Invariant,rows:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,row:&Value,fixture:Option<&Engine>,trace:Option<(&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,&[histories::Step])>,out:&mut Campaign){
    23	    let Some(other)=sample_rows(bound,rows,inv,row)else{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}.{}: column pair needs a second typed non-asset value",inv.table,inv.column.as_deref().unwrap_or("")));return;};
    24	    let altrow=other[&inv.table].iter().find(|r|r.field("id").ok()==row.field("id").ok()).unwrap();
    25	    let mut actors=histories::viewers(bound);
    26	    for (table,rs) in rows{for r in rs{for (c,k) in &bound.schema.tables[table].columns{if matches!(k.required(),C::Principal)&&let Ok(Value::Id(p))=r.field(c)&&!actors.contains(p){actors.push(p.clone());}}}}
    27	    for viewer in &actors{
    28	        if policy(bound,inv,rows,viewer,None,row)!=Ok(false)||policy(bound,inv,&other,viewer,None,altrow)!=Ok(false){continue;}
    29	        for bp in &bound.programs{
    30	            let p=&bp.program;
    31	            if !matches!(p.kind,ProgramKind::Query|ProgramKind::Mutation)||p.native{continue;}
    32	            let json=serde_json::to_string(p).unwrap();
    33	            if json.contains("NewId")||json.contains("Emit"){continue;}
    34	            if p.kind==ProgramKind::Mutation {
    35	                let writes=ir::write_tables(p);
    36	                let deps=inv.policy.as_ref().map(kernel::dependencies).unwrap_or_default();
    37	                if writes.iter().any(|(t,_)|t==&inv.table||deps.contains(t)){
    38	                    out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: column mutation can change protected row/policy before disclosure; boundary tracing required",p.name));continue;
    39	                }
    40	                if !writes.is_empty()&&(!bound.schema.maintains.is_empty()||bound.schema.tables.values().any(|t|t.on_delete.values().any(|a|a.writes()))){out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: implicit writes can change column policy before disclosure",p.name));continue;}
    41	            }
    42	            let args_list=histories::fixture_cases(&p.args,rows,&actors,out);
    43	            for args in args_list{
    44	                let execute=|alternate:bool|->Result<serde_json::Value,String>{
    45	                    let seeds=if alternate{&other}else{rows};
    46	                    let mut e=if let Some(fixture)=fixture{
    47	                        let mut e=fork(bound,fixture,Vec::new())?;
    48	                        if alternate{
    49	                            let t=&bound.schema.tables[&inv.table];
    50	                            let Value::Row(fields)=altrow else{return Err("not a row".into());};
    51	                            let clean=Value::Row(fields.iter().filter(|(c,_)|!t.engine_field(c)&&!t.recorded.contains_key(*c)&&!t.maintained.contains_key(*c)).map(|(c,v)|(c.clone(),v.clone())).collect());
    52	                            e.seed_at([(inv.table.clone(),clean)],NOW).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
    53	                        }e
    54	                    }else{engine(bound,seeds,program("$column.pair",ProgramKind::Query,vec![Stmt::Return(Expr::Literal(Value::Null))]))?};
    55	                    let actual=readback(&e)?;
    56	                    let current=actual.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten().find(|r|r.field("id").ok()==row.field("id").ok()).ok_or("column target is absent after validation")?;
    57	                    if policy(bound,inv,&actual,viewer,None,current)!=Ok(false){return Err("validated column-world policy is not false".into());}
    58	                    if alternate&&fixture.is_some(){
    59	                        for (table,rs) in rows.iter().filter(|(table,_)|bound.schema.maintain_for(table).is_none()){
    60	                            let definition=&bound.schema.tables[table];
    61	                            let same=rs.iter().all(|old|actual.get(table).into_iter().flatten().find(|r|r.field("id").ok()==old.field("id").ok()).is_some_and(|new|definition.columns.keys().filter(|c|!definition.maintained.contains_key(*c)&&!(table==&inv.table&&inv.column.as_ref()==Some(*c)&&old.field("id").ok()==row.field("id").ok())).all(|c|old.field(c).ok()==new.field(c).ok())));
    62	                            if !same{return Err(format!("alternate seed changes non-protected base fields in {table}; capture/recorded reduction unavailable"));}
    63	                        }
    64	                    }
    65	                    paired::observe(&mut e,p,viewer,args.clone())
    66	                };
    67	                let (a,b)=match (execute(false),execute(true)){
    68	                    (Ok(a),Ok(b))=>(a,b),
    69	                    (Err(why),_)|(_,Err(why))=>{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}.{} / {}: declined column world: {why}",inv.table,inv.column.as_deref().unwrap_or(""),p.name));continue;}
    70	                };
    71	                out.attempted+=2;*out.operations.entry(p.name.clone()).or_default()+=2;
    72	                if a==b{continue;}
    73	                if !matches!((execute(false),execute(true)),(Ok(aa),Ok(bb)) if aa==a&&bb==b){continue;}
    74	                out.violations.push(Counterexample{reachability:if fixture.is_some(){Reachability::AuthoredFixtureTrace}else{Reachability::SyntheticOperation},invariant_site:inv.site.to_string(),args:args.clone(),viewer:viewer.clone(),operation:p.name.clone(),effect:"column read observation".into(),row:row.clone(),old:Some(altrow.clone()),supporting_rows:trace.map(|(initial,_)|initial.clone()).unwrap_or_else(||rows.clone()),history:trace.map(|(_,steps)|steps.to_vec()).unwrap_or_default(),rule_path:format!("authored observer depends on {}.{} while policy is false in both typed worlds",inv.table,inv.column.as_deref().unwrap()),scope:if fixture.is_some(){"primary observation executes on trusted fixture; alternate column is a dependency test, not an authored alternate-history claim"}else{"schema-valid synthetic worlds; authored observer, no application reachability claim"}.into(),observations:Some(paired::Observations{present:a,absent:b,absent_rows:other.clone()})});
    75	                return;
    76	            }
    77	        }
    78	    }
    79	}
    80	pub(super) fn generated(bound:&Bound,out:&mut Campaign,sites:Option<&BTreeSet<String>>){
    81	    for inv in bound.invariants.iter().filter(|i|i.target=="read"&&i.column.is_some()&&sites.is_none_or(|s|s.contains(&i.site.to_string()))){
    82	        if inv.policy.as_ref().is_some_and(super::super::temporal::has_clock){out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}.{}: column clock pairs use the campaign clock only; other temporal ticks not covered",inv.table,inv.column.as_deref().unwrap()));}
    83	        for variant in 0..16{
    84	            let rows=match snapshot(bound,inv,variant){Ok(r)=>r,Err(why)=>{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: column snapshot: {why}",inv.table));continue;}};
    85	            let e=match engine(bound,&rows,program("$column.seed",ProgramKind::Query,vec![Stmt::Return(Expr::Literal(Value::Null))])){Ok(e)=>e,Err(why)=>{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: column seed: {why}",inv.table));continue;}};
    86	            let Ok(rows)=readback(&e)else{continue;};
    87	            for row in rows.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten(){run_pair(bound,inv,&rows,row,None,None,out);}
    88	            if out.violations.iter().any(|v|v.invariant_site==inv.site.to_string()){break;}
    89	        }
    90	    }
    91	}
    92	pub(super) fn fixture(bound:&Bound,fixture:&Engine,out:&mut Campaign){
    93	    let Ok(rows)=readback(fixture)else{return;};
    94	    for inv in bound.invariants.iter().filter(|i|i.target=="read"&&i.column.is_some()){
    95	        for row in rows.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten(){run_pair(bound,inv,&rows,row,Some(fixture),None,out);}
    96	    }
    97	}
    98	
    99	pub(super) fn after_history(bound:&Bound,fixture:&Engine,initial:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,steps:&[histories::Step],out:&mut Campaign){
   100	    let rows=match readback(fixture){Ok(r)=>r,Err(why)=>{out.unsupported.insert(why);return;}};
   101	    for inv in bound.invariants.iter().filter(|i|i.target=="read"&&i.column.is_some()){
   102	        if out.violations.iter().any(|v|v.invariant_site==inv.site.to_string()&&v.reachability==Reachability::AuthoredFixtureTrace&&!v.history.is_empty()){continue;}
   103	        for row in rows.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten(){run_pair(bound,inv,&rows,row,Some(fixture),Some((initial,steps)),out);}
   104	    }
   105	}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, Serialize, PartialEq, Eq)]
#[serde(rename_all="lowercase")]
pub enum Status { Proved, Refuted, Unproved }
#[derive(Clone, Debug, Serialize)]
pub struct Verdict {
    pub invariant: Invariant,
    pub status: Status,
    pub direct_rule: Status,
    pub paths: Vec<String>,
    pub reasons: Vec<String>,
    pub counterexample: Option<challenges::Counterexample>,
    pub obligations: Vec<Obligation>,
}
#[derive(Clone, Debug, Serialize)]
pub struct Report {
//! Lifetime-bound rule inputs, distinct from historical recorded attribution.
// @ref LLP 3000.014#4-the-capture — captures have their parent's type and activation lifecycle.
use super::*;

pub const PARENT_DEADLINE: &str = "$parentDeadline";
pub const DEADLINE: &str = "$deadline";
pub const CHILDREN: &str = "$children";
pub const LIVE_CHILDREN: &str = "$liveChildren";

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct Through {
    /// Bounded live maintenance; absence preserves captured wire bytes.
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub live: Option<u32>,
    pub reference: String,
    pub parent: String,
    /// Child engine field -> immediate parent field (including its captures).
    pub fields: BTreeMap<String, String>,
    pub identity: String,
    pub expiring: bool,
}

impl Table {
    pub fn engine_field(&self, field: &str) -> bool {
        (self.ephemeral.is_some() && field == DEADLINE) || self.recorded.contains_key(field)
            || self.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|t| {
                t.fields.contains_key(field) || matches!(field, DEADLINE | PARENT_DEADLINE)
            })
    }
    /// One classification for every expiry gate, including inherited deadlines.
    pub fn is_expiring(&self) -> bool {
        self.ephemeral.is_some() || self.expire.is_some() || self.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|t| t.expiring)
    }
    pub fn expiry_column(&self) -> Option<&str> {
        if self.ephemeral.is_some() || self.through.is_some() && self.is_expiring() {
            Some(DEADLINE)
        } else {
            self.expire.as_deref()
        }
    }
    pub fn deadline(&self, row: &Value) -> Option<i64> {
        match self
            .expiry_column()
            .and_then(|field| row_image(row, self).field(field).ok().cloned())
        {
            Some(Value::Int(at)) => Some(at),
            _ => None,
        }
    }
    /// Never trust a restored effective instant; recompute from immutable capture.
    pub fn recompute_deadline(&self, row: &mut Value) -> Result<(), crate::error::RuntimeError> {
        if self.through.is_none() {
            return Ok(());
        }
        let authored = self
            .expire
            .as_ref()
            .and_then(|c| match row_image(row, self).field(c) {
                Ok(Value::Int(at)) => Some(*at),
                _ => None,
            });
        let parent = match row.field(PARENT_DEADLINE)? {
            Value::Int(at) => Some(*at),
            Value::Null => None,
            _ => {
                return Err(crate::error::RuntimeError::Input(
                    "captured parent deadline must be a time or null".into(),
                ));
            }
        };
        let deadline = authored
            .into_iter()
            .chain(parent)
            .min()
            .map(Value::Int)
            .unwrap_or(Value::Null);
        if let Value::Row(fields) | Value::Map(fields) = row {
            fields.insert(DEADLINE.into(), deadline);
        }
        Ok(())
    }
}

impl RuleExpr {
    pub fn map_fields(&self, map: &impl Fn(&str) -> String) -> Self {
//! Server-authoritative lower row lifetime, independent of physical expiry.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.003#2-one-lifetime-predicate — every reader shares this half-open lifetime.
use super::*;

/// One opening is one system commit. Structural admission keeps that commit
/// inside the ordinary engine heap and event-count envelopes before any row
/// can enter the pending ledger.
pub(crate) const MAX_OPENING_EVENTS: u64 = 1_024;
pub(crate) const MAX_OPENING_BYTES: u64 = 16 << 20;

impl Table {
    /// Reads must classify/filter stored images before public actual-size
    /// accounting whenever either lifetime edge can hide one.
    pub fn read_lifetime_sensitive(&self) -> bool {
        self.not_before.is_some() || self.is_expiring()
    }

    pub fn opens_at(&self, row: &Value) -> Option<i64> {
        self.not_before
            .as_ref()
            .and_then(|field| match row.field(field) {
                Ok(Value::Int(at)) => Some(*at),
                Ok(_) => None,
                Err(_) => match self.defaults.get(field) {
                    Some(ColumnDefault::Literal(Value::Int(at))) => Some(*at),
                    _ => None,
                },
            })
    }
    pub fn opened(&self, row: &Value, now: i64) -> bool {
        self.opens_at(row).is_none_or(|at| at <= now)
    }
    pub fn live(&self, row: &Value, now: i64) -> bool {
        self.opened(row, now) && !self.expired(row, now)
    }
    pub fn opening_index(&self) -> Option<(&str, &Index)> {
        let column = self.not_before.as_deref()?;
        self.indexes
            .iter()
            .find(|(_, index)| {
                index.is_plain() && index.columns().first().map(String::as_str) == Some(column)
            })
            .map(|(name, index)| (name.as_str(), index))
    }
}

pub(super) fn validate(schema: &Schema, issues: &mut Vec<SchemaIssue>) {
    let timed = |name: &str| {
        schema
            .table(name)
            .is_some_and(|table| table.not_before.is_some())
    };
    for (name, table) in &schema.tables {
        let path = format!("tables.{name}");
        if table.not_before.is_some() && table.ephemeral.is_some() {
            issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: format!("{path}.ephemeral"), message:
                "ephemeral: not before cannot appear on an ephemeral table; keep the scheduled payload in a durable table".into() });
        }
        if let Some(through) = &table.through
            && (table.not_before.is_some() || timed(&through.parent))
        {
            issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: format!("{path}.notBefore"), message:
                format!("not before cannot compose with read through: {name} and {} need one captured opening boundary; use an explicit durable table", through.parent) });
        }
        if table.sync.as_ref().is_some_and(Sync::is_union) {
            let mut scopes = vec![name.as_str()];
            scopes.extend(
                table
                    .sync
                    .as_ref()
                    .into_iter()
                    .flat_map(Sync::arms)
                    .filter_map(|(audience, _)| match audience {
                        Audience::Target { table, .. } => Some(table.as_str()),
                        _ => None,
                    }),
            );
            if let Some(scope) = scopes.into_iter().find(|scope| timed(scope)) {
                issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: format!("{path}.sync"), message:
                    format!("union audiences cannot use a time audience: {scope} has `not before`; use `online only`") });
            }
        }
        if table.not_before.is_some() {
            let own = table
                .sync
                .as_ref()
                .map_or(0, |sync| sync.arms().count() as u64)
                + table
                    .written_by
                    .values()
                    .filter(|authority| !authority.audience)
                    .count() as u64;
            let memberships = schema.tables.values().flat_map(|content| content.sync.iter().flat_map(Sync::arms))
                .filter(|(audience, _)| matches!(audience, Audience::Target { table: target, .. } if target == name))
                .count() as u64;
            let events = own.saturating_add(memberships);
            let owner_slots = table
                .written_by
                .values()
                .filter(|authority| !authority.audience)
                .count() as u64;
            let mut read_probes = Vec::new();
            crate::engine::rules::probe_sites(&table.rules.read, &mut read_probes);
            // The opening row is decoded before publication, including for an
            // online-only table which emits no event. A join carries a subset
            // of that row's bounded fields plus less framing than this same
            // envelope. Reserve at least the base decode and one envelope for
            // every possible event.
            let bytes = crate::rows::disclosed::decode_reservation(table)
                .saturating_mul(events.max(1));
            if events > MAX_OPENING_EVENTS || bytes > MAX_OPENING_BYTES {
                issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: format!("{path}.notBefore"), message: format!(
                    "not before opening fanout is too large for one bounded publication commit ({events} events, {bytes} reserved bytes); split this audience into smaller tables"
                ) });
            }
            // Opening runs as the system principal, so its recipient-specific
            // private-field projection cannot safely borrow the caller's
            // bounded authorization walk or scratch. Keep that projection
            // local to the opening row; ordinary rows may still use witnesses.
            if owner_slots > 0 && !read_probes.is_empty() {
                issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: format!("{path}.notBefore"), message: format!(
                    "not before with private `written by` fields needs a probe-free read rule so opening publication has no unbounded witness walk; use local field comparisons, declare the fields readable by audience, or remove `not before`"
                ) });
            }
        }
    }
    for (position, maintain) in schema.maintains.iter().enumerate() {
        let timed_row_key = maintain
            .row_key
            .as_ref()
            .is_some_and(|row_key| timed(&row_key.table));
        if timed(&maintain.source) || timed(&maintain.target) || timed_row_key {
            issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: format!("maintains[{position}]"), message:
                format!("maintain {} cannot use `not before` on its source, target, or row-key member table until boundary recounts are supported", maintain.target) });
        }
    }
    for timeline in &schema.timelines {
        if let Some(table) = timeline
            .event_tables()
            .into_iter()
            .find(|table| timed(table))
        {
            issues.push(SchemaIssue {
                path: format!("timelines.{}", timeline.name),
                message: format!(
                    "timeline {} cannot map time-audience table {table}; query the table directly",
                    timeline.name
                ),
            });
        }
    }
}
                for child in group {
                    let current = self.metered_scan(|runner, allowance| runner.tx.get(allowance, r.child, &child))?;
                    if let Some(current) = current.filter(|row| row.field(r.column).is_ok_and(|v| v == id)) {
                        self.clear_reference(r.child, r.column, current)?;
                    }
                }
            }
        }
        // 6. Delete the closure, children first, each from its current image.
        let reached: Vec<(String, String)> = visits[1..].iter().map(|(t, id)| Ok((t.clone(), id.as_str()?.to_owned()))).collect::<Result<_, RuntimeError>>()?;
        drop(visits);
        for (table, id) in deletion_order(schema, &reached) {
            let current = self.metered_scan(|runner, allowance| runner.tx.get(allowance, &table, &id))?;
            let Some(current) = current else { continue };
            self.delete_reached(&table, current)?;
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Streams the ids of the live rows referencing `id` through `r` to
    /// `visit`, through its index, until `visit` answers false. A capped walk
    /// (cascade, set null) reads `PAGE` rows per seek and holds at most the
    /// reference's cap; a restrict probe reads one row per seek, so it examines
    /// nothing past its witness. Images are dropped before `visit` runs. Seeks
    /// start at the live suffix, so an expired backlog is never visited.
    fn each_child(&mut self, r: &Referencing<'_>, id: &Value, capped: bool, visit: &mut dyn FnMut(&mut Self, Value) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let schema = self.schema;
        let index = schema.reference_index(r.child, r.column).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("{}.{} has no reference index", r.child, r.column)))?.to_owned();
        let (cap, page_rows) = if capped { (schema.reference_cap(r.child, r.column).map_or(u64::MAX, u64::from), PAGE) } else { (u64::MAX, 1) };
        // @ref LLP 3000.039#2.3 — seek on the effective deadline (a read-through child's `$deadline`), so no expired row is visited.
        let ranges = if r.table.expiry_column().is_some() && !through_reference(r) {
            let now = self.context.now.ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Input("delete clock is not pinned".into()))?;
            vec![(vec![id.clone(), Value::Null], None), (vec![id.clone()], Some(Value::Int(now)))]
        } else {
            vec![(vec![id.clone()], None)]
        };
        let mut live = 0_u64;
        for (prefix, gt) in ranges {
            let mut bounds = Bounds { gt, ..Default::default() };
            loop {
                let page = self.metered_scan(|runner, allowance| runner.tx.scan(allowance, r.child, &index, &prefix, &bounds, Dir::Asc, page_rows))?;
                let more = page.len() == page_rows;
                if let Some((_, key)) = page.last() { bounds.after = crate::key::decode(key).map(|v| v.into_iter().skip(prefix.len()).collect()); }
                let mut ids = Vec::with_capacity(page.len());
                for (row, _) in page {
                    if self.device {
                        // @ref LLP 3000.039#6.4 — a device predicts only over current rows: retained history queues the delete, and only a live, current (or own provisional) row is a witness.
                        // A retained, no longer current row is work the reservation does not
                        // price and a witness the device cannot judge: the delete queues.
                        if self.tx.retained_row(r.child, &row)? && !self.tx.current_row(r.child, &row)? {
                            return Err(RuntimeError::Prediction(format!("deleting meets retained {} history that references it; queued without a local delete", r.child)));
                        }
                        if self.expired(r.table, &row) || !(self.tx.current_row(r.child, &row)? || self.is_provisional(r.child, &row)?) {
                            return Err(RuntimeError::Prediction(format!("deleting needs a current image of every {} row that references it; queued without a local delete", r.child)));
                        }
                    } else if self.expired(r.table, &row) { continue; }

pub fn dependencies(rule: &R) -> BTreeSet<String> {
    let mut out=BTreeSet::new();
    match rule {
        R::Exists{table,..}|R::Created{table,..} => {out.insert(table.clone());}
        R::And(xs)|R::Or(xs) => for x in xs {out.extend(dependencies(x));}, R::Not(x) => out.extend(dependencies(x)),
        R::Eq(a,b)|R::Ne(a,b)|R::Compare(_,a,b) => for t in [a,b] { for (table,_) in t.references() {out.insert(table.into());} },
        _=>{}
    }
    out
}

//! Server-authoritative lower row lifetime, independent of physical expiry.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.003#2-one-lifetime-predicate — every reader shares this half-open lifetime.
use super::*;

/// One opening is one system commit. Structural admission keeps that commit
/// inside the ordinary engine heap and event-count envelopes before any row
/// can enter the pending ledger.
pub(crate) const MAX_OPENING_EVENTS: u64 = 1_024;
pub(crate) const MAX_OPENING_BYTES: u64 = 16 << 20;

impl Table {
    /// Reads must classify/filter stored images before public actual-size
    /// accounting whenever either lifetime edge can hide one.
    pub fn read_lifetime_sensitive(&self) -> bool {
        self.not_before.is_some() || self.is_expiring()
    }

    pub fn opens_at(&self, row: &Value) -> Option<i64> {
        self.not_before
            .as_ref()
            .and_then(|field| match row.field(field) {
                Ok(Value::Int(at)) => Some(*at),
                Ok(_) => None,
                Err(_) => match self.defaults.get(field) {
                    Some(ColumnDefault::Literal(Value::Int(at))) => Some(*at),
                    _ => None,
                },
            })
    }
    pub fn opened(&self, row: &Value, now: i64) -> bool {
        self.opens_at(row).is_none_or(|at| at <= now)
    }
    pub fn live(&self, row: &Value, now: i64) -> bool {
        self.opened(row, now) && !self.expired(row, now)
    }
    pub fn opening_index(&self) -> Option<(&str, &Index)> {
        let column = self.not_before.as_deref()?;

                index.is_plain() && index.columns().first().map(String::as_str) == Some(column)
            })

Two source paths can produce false reachable refutations: a column mutation whose non-rule policy dependencies are treated as empty, and a deletion judged before an earlier cascade authorizes it. I’m checking the delegation and revocation lints for any additional material finding.

codex
NOT READY

1. **P1 — Column mutations treat unknown policy dependencies as empty.** [column_reads.rs:36](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-sec3/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/security/challenges/column_reads.rs:36) permits mutations when `inv.policy` is absent, although the concrete AST evaluator can evaluate aggregate policies. For `count(roles[principal = viewer, enabled = true]) = 1`, a mutation can enable the caller before returning the protected column. Comparing outputs against the initial false policy then produces a false reachable refutation of an authorized disclosure. **Repair:** decline mutation observers without complete policy dependencies, or establish dependency stability from the retained AST.

2. **P1 — Delete assertions miss implicit policy changes earlier in the operation.** [histories.rs:101](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-sec3/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/security/challenges/histories.rs:101) checks only explicit dependency writes before judging removed pre-images against the operation’s initial state. Consider deleting a parent that cascades away the caller’s ban, then deleting a note whose delete rule and invariant both require `not exists bans[viewer]`. The note deletion is authorized at its boundary, but this verifier reports a reachable violation because the ban existed initially. **Repair:** account for transitive implicit writes when establishing stability; otherwise decline the assertion pending executed-effect tracing. Apply the same repair to update/insert checks.

3. **P2 — Defaulted roles bypass escalation advice.** [lints.rs:93](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-sec3/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/security/lints.rs:93) requires the role column to appear explicitly in the authored write. A self-enrollment mutation inserting only `{ principal: viewer }` therefore escapes `N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION` when the role defaults to an authority-bearing value such as `'admin'`, even when later rules trust that role. **Repair:** analyze effective inserted role values, including defaults, through the per-operation delegation gates.

NOT READY

1. **P1 — Column mutations treat unknown policy dependencies as empty.** [column_reads.rs:36](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-sec3/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/security/challenges/column_reads.rs:36) permits mutations when `inv.policy` is absent, although the concrete AST evaluator can evaluate aggregate policies. Comparing outputs against the initial false policy then produces a false reachable refutation of an authorized disclosure. **Repair:** decline mutation observers without complete policy dependencies, or establish dependency stability from the retained AST.

2. **P1 — Delete assertions miss implicit policy changes earlier in the operation.** [histories.rs:101](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-sec3/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/security/challenges/histories.rs:101) checks only explicit dependency writes before judging removed pre-images against the operation’s initial state. Consider deleting a parent that cascades away the caller’s ban, then deleting a note whose delete rule and invariant both require `not exists bans[viewer]`. The note deletion is authorized at its boundary, but this verifier reports a reachable violation because the ban existed initially. **Repair:** account for transitive implicit writes when establishing stability; otherwise decline the assertion pending executed-effect tracing. 3. **P2 — Defaulted roles bypass escalation advice.** [lints.rs:93](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-sec3/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/security/lints.rs:93) requires the role column to appear explicitly in the authored write. A self-enrollment mutation inserting only `{ principal: viewer }` therefore escapes `N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION` when the role defaults to an authority-bearing value such as `'admin'`, even when later rules trust that role. **Repair:** analyze effective inserted role values, including defaults, through the per-operation delegation gates.
